#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Design and Implement Scenario Matrix Configuration Schema and Validation`

#### **2. Description**

To enable advanced, multi-variable testing of agent performance, we need a robust way to define a "matrix" of test configurations. This ticket covers the creation of the YAML configuration file that will drive the new Scenario Matrix Runner.

The goal is to design a schema that is both powerful and easy for developers to understand. This configuration will specify a base scenario and a set of parameters to vary, allowing the system to automatically generate and run all possible combinations.

This work is foundational for the entire Scenario Testing and Reporting Framework and does not include the logic for actually running the scenarios, only for defining and validating their configuration.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **YAML Schema Definition:**
   * A formal YAML schema is defined and documented. The configuration file (e.g., `matrix.scenario.yaml`) must support the following top-level keys:
     * `name` (string, required): A human-readable name for the test matrix (e.g., "GitHub Issue Prompt Robustness").
     * `description` (string, optional): A longer description of the test's goals.
     * `base_scenario` (string, required): The file path (relative to the project root) to the base `.scenario.yaml` file to be used in each run.
     * `runs_per_combination` (integer, optional, default: `1`): The number of times to execute each unique combination of parameters to check for consistency.
     * `matrix` (array, required): An array of objects defining the axes of variation. Each object must contain:
       * `parameter` (string, required): The target field in the base scenario to override, specified using dot-notation (e.g., `character.llm.model`, `run[0].input`).
       * `values` (array, required): A list of the values to be substituted for the specified `parameter`.
2. **TypeScript Interfaces:**
   * A new file, `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/matrix-schema.ts`, will be created.
   * This file will contain exported TypeScript interfaces (`MatrixConfig`, `MatrixAxis`, etc.) that precisely match the defined YAML schema, ensuring strong type safety for the rest of the feature.
3. **Configuration Validation:**
   * The `matrix-schema.ts` file will also contain an exported validation function, e.g., `validateMatrixConfig(config: unknown): MatrixConfig`.
   * This function will use a library like **Zod** to rigorously validate the parsed YAML object against the schema.
   * Validation errors must be user-friendly and clearly indicate the specific problem in the configuration file (e.g., `Validation Error: "matrix[1].values" must be a non-empty array.`).
4. **Documentation:**
   * A new documentation file, `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/docs/matrix-testing.md`, will be created.
   * This document will explain the purpose of matrix testing and provide a detailed guide on how to create the `matrix.scenario.yaml` file, with clear explanations for each field.

#### **4. Example Configuration**

The documentation and any tests should include a clear example, like the one below:

```yaml
# matrix.scenario.yaml
name: "GitHub Issue Action Chaining Analysis"
description: "Tests the reliability of listing GitHub issues under various LLM and prompt configurations."
base_scenario: "packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/examples/test-github-issues.scenario.yaml"
runs_per_combination: 3
matrix:
  - parameter: "character.llm.model"
    values:
      - "gpt-4-turbo"
      - "gpt-3.5-turbo"
  - parameter: "run[0].input"
    values:
      - "List open issues for elizaOS/eliza"
      - "Find current issues for the elizaos/eliza repo"
      - "Show me what's open in the elizaOS/eliza GitHub."
```

* The implementation of the `elizaos scenario matrix` CLI command itself.

**ID:** `FEAT-134` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Performance Reporting Dashboard`

**Tags:** `cli`, `reporting`,- `feature`, `pdf`

**Estimated Story Points:** `5`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-133` (Dynamic Report Rendering)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Add PDF export functionality to the 'report generate' command`

#### **2. Description**

To enhance the shareability and archiving of performance reports, this ticket adds an optional PDF export feature. It introduces a `--format pdf` flag to the `elizaos report generate` command, which will generate a high-fidelity PDF version of the HTML report.

This will be achieved by using a headless browser library, **Puppeteer**, to "print" the final, fully-rendered HTML report to a PDF file. This approach ensures that the PDF is a perfect, pixel-for-pixel representation of the interactive web report, including all charts and dynamic content.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **CLI Flag Implementation:**
   * The `elizaos report generate` command is updated to accept a new optional flag: `--format <format>`.
   * The accepted values for `<format>` are `html` (default) and `pdf`.
   * The command's help text is updated to document the new flag.
2. **Puppeteer Integration:**
   * The `puppeteer` npm package is added as a development dependency to the `packages/cli` project.
   * A new utility or service is created to encapsulate the PDF generation logic.
3. **PDF Generation Logic:**
   * When `--format pdf` is specified, the command first generates the final HTML report in a temporary location.
   * It then launches a headless Chrome instance using Puppeteer.
   * It navigates to the temporary HTML file using a `file://` URL.
   * It waits for the page and all JavaScript (including chart animations) to be fully loaded and rendered. A delay or a specific DOM-ready event might be necessary.
   * It calls Puppeteer's `page.pdf()` method to generate the PDF.
   * The generated PDF is saved to the specified output path (e.g., `<input_dir>/performance_report.pdf`).
   * The temporary HTML file is deleted after the PDF is successfully created.
4. **Print-Friendly Styling:**
   * The HTML template is updated with a `@media print` CSS block.
   * These print-specific styles will ensure the PDF output is optimized for a document format:
     * Remove unnecessary UI elements like interactive search/sort buttons from the print view.
     * Ensure chart animations are disabled for printing to capture their final state.
     * Optimize page breaks to avoid splitting charts or tables awkwardly across pages.
     * Set a consistent page margin and font size for readability.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify / Create:**

* `packages/cli/package.json`: Add `puppeteer` dependency.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/generate.ts`: Add the `--format` flag and the conditional logic to invoke the PDF generator.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/src/pdf-generator.ts`: New file containing the Puppeteer logic.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/src/assets/report_template.html`: Add the `@media print` styles.

**Example PDF Generation Logic (**`pdf-generator.ts`):

```typescript
import puppeteer from 'puppeteer';
import fs from 'fs/promises';
import path from 'path';
export async function generatePdfFromHtml(htmlContent: string, outputPath: string): Promise<void> {
  const tempHtmlPath = path.join(path.dirname(outputPath), 'temp_report.html');
  await fs.writeFile(tempHtmlPath, htmlContent);
  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ headless: true });
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  
  await page.goto(`file://${tempHtmlPath}`, { waitUntil: 'networkidle0' });
  // Optional: Wait for charts to finish animating
  await page.waitForTimeout(2000); 
  await page.pdf({
    path: outputPath,
    format: 'A4',
    printBackground: true,
    margin: { top: '20px', right: '20px', bottom: '20px', left: '20px' }
  });
  await browser.close();
  await fs.unlink(tempHtmlPath);
}
```

**Example Print-Specific CSS:**

```css
@media print {
  body {
    font-size: 10pt;
  }
  .no-print, .interactive-controls {
    display: none !important;
  }
  canvas {
    /* Ensure charts are not split across pages */
    page-break-inside: avoid;
  }
}
```

* Advanced PDF features like an interactive table of contents or bookmarks.
* Support for any other export formats besides `html` and `pdf`.1b:T1b4d,# Dynamic Prompting Implementation for ElizaOS Scenarios

## Overview

Implement **Dynamic Prompting** (multi-turn conversations) in ElizaOS scenarios to enable sophisticated testing of agent behavior through extended conversations where an LLM simulates realistic user responses.

## Problem Statement

Current ElizaOS scenarios are limited to single-turn interactions, making it impossible to test:

* Multi-step problem solving
* Context retention across conversation turns
* Clarification and follow-up question handling
* Natural conversation flow assessment
* Error recovery and correction flows

## Solution

Extend the existing scenario framework to support multi-turn conversations where:

1. **Agent** receives initial user input
2. **Agent** responds with thoughts, actions, and replies
3. **LLM Simulator** generates realistic user follow-up based on agent response
4. **Agent** continues the conversation based on simulated user input
5. Process repeats for specified number of turns or until conditions are met

## Key Requirements

### 1\. Backward Compatibility

* **100% backward compatible** - all existing scenarios must work unchanged
* Gradual adoption path for teams to enhance existing scenarios
* No breaking changes to existing APIs or CLI commands

### 2\. Core Components

#### Schema Extensions

* Extend `RunStepSchema` with optional `conversation` field
* Add new evaluation types: `conversation_length`, `conversation_flow`, `user_satisfaction`, `context_retention`
* Support conversation configuration with user simulator settings

#### User Simulator

* LLM-based response generation with persona-driven prompts
* Configurable personality, objectives, constraints, and knowledge level
* Realistic conversation progression based on agent responses

#### Conversation Manager

* Multi-turn execution orchestration
* Termination condition checking (satisfaction, solution provided, escalation needed)
* Turn-level and final evaluation support
* Conversation transcript generation

#### New Evaluators

* **Conversation Length**: Validate optimal conversation duration
* **Conversation Flow**: Detect required conversation patterns
* **User Satisfaction**: Measure user satisfaction through sentiment analysis
* **Context Retention**: Verify agent memory across conversation turns

### 3\. Configuration Examples

#### Basic Multi-Turn Conversation

```yaml
run:
  - input: "I need help with something"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 4
      user_simulator:
        persona: "polite customer with a billing question"
        objective: "find out why charged twice this month"
        temperature: 0.6
      final_evaluations:
        - type: "llm_judge"
          prompt: "Did the agent successfully help resolve the billing issue?"
          expected: "yes"
```

#### Advanced Persona-Driven Conversation

```yaml
run:
  - input: "This is ridiculous! Your product doesn't work!"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 6
      user_simulator:
        persona: "angry customer who had bad experience"
        objective: "vent frustration but eventually want help"
        style: "initially hostile, gradually becomes cooperative if handled well"
        constraints:
          - "Start with complaints and criticism"
          - "Don't accept first solution immediately"
          - "Become more cooperative if agent shows empathy"
      termination_conditions:
        - type: "user_expresses_satisfaction"
        - type: "agent_escalates_to_human"
```

#### **Description**

This ticket creates the foundational entry point for the Scenario Runner within the ElizaOS CLI. The goal is to register a new top-level `elizaos scenario` command with a `run` subcommand. This command will be responsible for parsing command-line arguments and will serve as the starting point for all future scenario execution logic.

For this initial implementation, the command will perform a "smoke test": it will parse the provided YAML file path, read the file, and print its contents to the console to verify that the command structure and argument handling are working correctly.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new top-level command `scenario` is available in the `elizaos` CLI.
2. The `scenario` command has a subcommand named `run`.
3. Running `elizaos scenario run <path/to/scenario.yaml>` successfully reads the specified YAML file and prints its parsed JSON representation to the console.
4. The `run` subcommand accepts a `--live` boolean flag (which will not have any logic yet, but must be recognized).
5. Running `elizaos scenario run --help` displays help text for the command, its positional argument (`filePath`), and its options (`--live`).
6. If the file path argument is missing or the file doesn't exist, the command exits gracefully with an informative error message.

#### **Technical Approach**

The CLI application in `packages/cli` uses the `yargs` library to define and manage commands. We will follow the existing pattern to add our new command.

**1. Create the Command Handler File**

First, create a new file to encapsulate the logic for the `scenario` command.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

**2. Implement the Command Logic**

Inside `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`, we will define the command structure and its handler. We will also need to add a YAML parsing library.

* **Add dependency**: From the root of the `eliza` monorepo, run `bun add js-yaml` to make it available to the CLI package.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
import type { Argv } from 'yargs';
import yaml from 'js-yaml';
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { elizaLogger } from '@elizaos/core'; // Assuming a shared logger
// Define the arguments for the 'run' subcommand
interface ScenarioRunArgs {
  filePath: string;
  live: boolean;
}
// Command definition for 'scenario run'
export const command = 'scenario <command>';
export const desc = 'Manage and execute ElizaOS scenarios.';
export const builder = (yargs: Argv): Argv => {
  return yargs.command(
    'run <filePath>',
    'Execute a scenario from a YAML file.',
    (yargs: Argv) => {
      return yargs
        .positional('filePath', {
          describe: 'Path to the .scenario.yaml file',
          type: 'string',
          demandOption: true,
        })
        .option('live', {
          alias: 'l',
          type: 'boolean',
          description: 'Run scenario in live mode, ignoring mocks.',
          default: false,
        });
    },
    (argv: ScenarioRunArgs) => {
      // This is the handler function that executes when the command is called
      handleRunScenario(argv);
    }
  );
};
// The core logic for the command
function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs) {
  elizaLogger.info(`Starting scenario run with args: ${JSON.stringify(args)}`);
  try {
    const fullPath = path.resolve(args.filePath);
    elizaLogger.info(`Attempting to read scenario file from: ${fullPath}`);
    if (!fs.existsSync(fullPath)) {
      elizaLogger.error(`Error: File not found at '${fullPath}'`);
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const fileContents = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf8');
    const scenario = yaml.load(fileContents);
    console.log('--- Parsed Scenario Content ---');
    console.log(JSON.stringify(scenario, null, 2));
    console.log('-----------------------------');
    
    elizaLogger.info('Scenario file parsed successfully.');
  } catch (error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}
```

**3. Register the New Command**

Now, we need to wire our new command module into the main CLI entry point.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/cli.ts` (or the equivalent main `yargs` file where other commands are registered).

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/cli.ts
// ... other imports
import * as scenario from './commands/scenario'; // Import the new module
// ... inside the main yargs setup function
// Find where other .command() calls are made, and add this one.
// It will likely be chained with the others.
yargs
  .command(agent)
  .command(test)
  .command(scenario) // Add our new scenario command module here
  // ... other commands and configurations
```

#### **Testing Strategy**

1. Create a simple `test.scenario.yaml` file in the root of the project:

   ```yaml
   name: "Initial Test Scenario"
   description: "A simple file for testing the CLI scaffolding."
   ```
2. From the `packages/cli` directory, build the CLI changes: `bun run build`.
3. Run the command from the project root and verify the output:

   ```bash
   # Test basic execution
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./test.scenario.yaml
   # Expected output: The JSON representation of the YAML file printed to the console.
   # Test the --live flag
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./test.scenario.yaml --live
   # Expected output: The log line should show "live": true in the arguments.
   # Test help command
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run --help
   # Expected output: Help text showing options for the 'run' command.
   # Test file not found error
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./non-existent-file.yaml
   # Expected output: A clear error message "File not found" and a non-zero exit code.

\### Summary

Windows CI tests are failing due to incorrect path handling when running \`elizaos agent start\` with the \`--path\` flag. The file path is being malformed, resulting in doubled/concatenated paths with embedded quotes.

\### Error Details

The test command:

\`\`\`bash

elizaos agent start --remote-url http://localhost:3000 --path "D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

Results in this malformed path:

\`\`\`

D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\"D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

Error message:

\`\`\`

error: File not found at path: D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\"D:\\a\\eliza\\eliza\\packages\\cli\\tests\\test-characters\\max.json"

\`\`\`

\### Root Cause

The path resolution in \`packages/cli/src/commands/agent.ts\` (around the \`startAgent\` function) is not properly handling quoted paths on Windows. When \`path.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path)\` is called, it's incorrectly concatenating the current working directory with an already-absolute path.

\### Affected Code

\- File: \`packages/cli/dist/chunk-KKAK7OQA.js\` (compiled from source)

\- Line: Around line 3326 where \`path12.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path)\` is called

\- Source likely in: \`packages/cli/src/commands/agent.ts\` or related agent command files

\### Reproduction

Only occurs on Windows systems when running:

\`\`\`bash

bun test packages/cli/tests/create-commands.test.ts

\`\`\`

\### Proposed Fix

1\. Check if the provided path is already absolute before calling \`path.resolve()\`

2\. Handle Windows path separators and quoted paths correctly

3\. Add unit tests specifically for Windows path scenarios

\### Example Fix

\`\`\`typescript

*// Instead of:*

const filePath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), options.path);

*// Use:*

const inputPath = options.path.replace(/^"|"$/g, ''); *// Remove surrounding quotes*

const filePath = path.isAbsolute(inputPath)

? inputPath

: path.resolve(process.cwd(), inputPath);

\`\`\`

\### Priority

High - Blocking CI/CD pipeline

\### Labels

\- bug

\- windows

```20:Te73,### Problem Statement
Currently, ElizaOS scenario testing lacks the ability to mock internal agent runtime calls (particularly LLM interactions) when testing via the API client. This makes it difficult to create deterministic tests for agent behavior and workflows.

### Current Limitations
- Global function mocks only work for code executed in the same process
- No way to intercept `runtime.useModel` calls from the bootstrap plugin
- Agent behavior is non-deterministic due to real LLM calls
- Difficult to test specific agent decision paths and responses

### Why Different Approach Was Needed

The existing mocking system was designed for **code execution environments** (E2B sandbox and local `node -e` processes), where mocks are applied to the execution context. However, **agent testing** works differently:

- **E2B/Local Execution**: Code runs in isolated processes/sandboxes where global mocks can be applied
- **Agent Testing**: Agent runs as a service with API client interaction, requiring direct runtime method interception
- **Architecture Gap**: The bootstrap plugin calls `runtime.useModel` internally, which can't be mocked through the execution environment approach

This architectural difference necessitated a new approach that directly intercepts runtime methods rather than relying on execution context mocking.

### Proposed Solution: Runtime Method Mocking

We've implemented a new mocking approach that directly intercepts `IAgentRuntime` method calls:

#### Key Components

1. **MockEngine Enhancement**
   - Added `runtimeMethodMocks` to support mocking runtime methods
   - Priority system: `runtimeMethod` > `service` > `globalFunction`
   - Direct method replacement on the runtime instance

2. **Schema Updates**
   - Extended `MockSchema` to include `runtimeMethod: z.string().optional()`
   - Supports mocking any `IAgentRuntime` method (e.g., `useModel`, `getService`)

3. **Conditional Matching**
   - Matcher functions can inspect method arguments
   - Example: `"return args[1] && args[1].prompt && args[1].prompt.includes('should respond')"`
   - Supports complex conditional logic for when to apply mocks

#### Usage Example

```yaml
setup:
  mocks:
    - runtimeMethod: "useModel"
      method: "useModel"
      when:
        matcher: "return args[1] && args[1].prompt && args[1].prompt.includes('should respond')"
      response: "<response><name>agent</name><reasoning>Mocked response</reasoning><action>RESPOND</action></response>"
```

#### Benefits

1. **Deterministic Testing**: Replace LLM calls with static responses
2. **Full Workflow Testing**: Test complete agent decision flows
3. **Conditional Mocking**: Apply mocks based on specific conditions
4. **Runtime Integration**: Works seamlessly with existing agent architecture
5. **Backward Compatibility**: Existing mocks continue to work

#### Implementation Steps

- MockEngine runtime method support
- Schema updates for runtimeMethod mocks
- Conditional matching with matcher functions
- Integration with scenario runner
- Basic useModel mocking working
- Add support for mocking other runtime methods (getService, etc.)
- Create comprehensive test scenarios
- Add documentation and examples
- Consider adding mock recording/replay capabilities

### Technical Details

The implementation works by:
1. Creating the `IAgentRuntime` instance in the scenario runner
2. Applying mocks directly to the runtime object before agent initialization
3. The bootstrap plugin receives the mocked runtime and uses the mocked methods
4. Mocks are reverted after scenario completion

This approach provides the foundation for robust, deterministic agent testing while maintaining the existing architecture.21:Tb74,# feat(scenarios): Add Step Count Evaluator

Links: [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726)

## Summary
Add an evaluator that asserts on the number of agent/tool/action steps taken to complete a scenario step. This encourages concise trajectories and prevents thrashing.

## Goals
- Count actions/tools invoked during response generation for a step
- Assert against `max_steps`, `min_steps`, optional `target_steps`

## Acceptance Criteria
1. New evaluator type `step_count`
2. Step count derived from trajectory/action memories queried via `AgentRuntime.getMemories`
3. Evaluator passes if within bounds and returns a clear message

## Schema Changes
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`:

```ts
const StepCountEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('step_count'),
  max_steps: z.number().optional(),
  min_steps: z.number().optional(),
  target_steps: z.number().optional(),
  // optional filter by action name prefix to scope counting
  action_prefix: z.string().optional(),
});
```

## Evaluation Implementation
Leverage the existing `TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator` patterns.

```ts
class StepCountEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params: EvaluationSchema, _runResult: ExecutionResult, runtime: AgentRuntime): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'step_count') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    try {
      const memories = await runtime.getMemories({
        tableName: 'messages', agentId: runtime.agentId, count: 200, unique: false,
      });
      const actionResults = memories.filter(m => m?.type === 'messages' && m.content?.type === 'action_result');
      const filtered = params.action_prefix
        ? actionResults.filter(m => (m.content?.actionName ?? '').startsWith(params.action_prefix!))
        : actionResults;
      const steps = filtered.length;

      const tooMany = params.max_steps != null && steps > params.max_steps;
      const tooFew = params.min_steps != null && steps < params.min_steps;
      const success = !(tooMany || tooFew);
      return {
        success,
        message: `Steps taken: ${steps} (min=${params.min_steps ?? '-'}, target=${params.target_steps ?? '-'}, max=${params.max_steps ?? '-'})`,
      };
    } catch (error: any) {
      return { success: false, message: `Step count evaluation failed: ${error.message ?? String(error)}` };
    }
  }
}
```

Register in `EvaluationEngine` constructor:

```ts
this.register('step_count', new StepCountEvaluator());
```

## Example Usage

```yaml
evaluations:
  - type: step_count
    max_steps: 3
    action_prefix: "github-service."
```

## Tests
- Unit: evaluator result on synthetic memories
- Integration: scenario that triggers 1-2 simple actions and validates bound

## Notes
- Future enhancement: count only steps within the current scenario step window by tagging messages with a scenario/step id context field.


22:Tdbc,# feat(scenarios): Add Consistency Evaluator

Links: [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726)

## Summary
Add an evaluator that runs the same step multiple times and asserts consistency over a chosen metric (response content, length, execution time, token counts). Useful for detecting non-determinism and regression in prompt/rules.

## Goals
- Re-run a step N times
- Compare result metrics across runs
- Allow tolerance configuration

## Acceptance Criteria
1. New evaluator type `consistency_check`
2. Supports metrics: `response_length` | `execution_time` | `token_count`
3. Configurable `runs` (>=2) and `tolerance` (absolute or percentage)
4. Consolidated pass/fail with clear variance message

## Schema Changes
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`:

```ts
const ConsistencyEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('consistency_check'),
  runs: z.number().min(2),
  metric: z.enum(['response_length', 'execution_time', 'token_count']),
  tolerance: z.number().min(0), // if < 1 treat as proportion, else absolute
});
```

## Orchestration Changes
Add a helper to re-execute the current step within the selected environment provider without re-running the entire scenario:

- Expose a `runSingleStep(step, scenarioContext)` on providers that mirrors the logic in `run`, returning an `ExecutionResult`.
- In `EvaluationEngine`, when encountering `consistency_check`, call this helper to collect multiple `ExecutionResult`s for the same step.
- Fallback: If helper is not implemented, re-run `run([step])` via a shim.

## Evaluation Implementation

```ts
class ConsistencyEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  constructor(private rerunStep: (times: number) => Promise<ExecutionResult[]>) {}

  async evaluate(params: EvaluationSchema, runResult: ExecutionResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'consistency_check') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');

    const runs = await this.rerunStep(params.runs);
    const values = runs.map(r => {
      switch (params.metric) {
        case 'response_length': return r.stdout.length;
        case 'execution_time': return r.durationMs ?? 0;
        case 'token_count': {
          const llm = r.metrics?.llm ?? [];
          return llm.reduce((sum, m) => sum + (m.totalTokens ?? (m.promptTokens ?? 0) + (m.completionTokens ?? 0)), 0);
        }
      }
    });

    const min = Math.min(...values);
    const max = Math.max(...values);
    const spread = max - min;
    const baseline = values[0] || 1;
    const proportion = baseline ? spread / baseline : spread;
    const threshold = params.tolerance < 1 ? params.tolerance : params.tolerance / baseline;
    const success = proportion <= threshold;

    return {
      success,
      message: `Consistency ${success ? 'ok' : 'failed'} for ${params.metric}: min=${min}, max=${max}, spread=${spread}, tolerance=${params.tolerance}`,
    };
  }
}
```

Register in `EvaluationEngine` with a provider-specific `rerunStep` function wired from the scenario runner.

## Example Usage

```yaml
evaluations:
  - type: consistency_check
    runs: 3
    metric: execution_time
    tolerance: 0.2   # 20% spread allowed
```

## Tests
- Unit: evaluator math on synthetic values
- Integration: deterministic small prompt with low tolerance; non-deterministic with higher tolerance

## Notes
- This evaluator requires access to the step context. Implementation will pass a `rerunStep(times)` closure into the evaluator at construction time.


23:Tc5e,# feat(scenarios): Add Cost Evaluator

Links: [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726)

## Summary
Introduce an evaluator that asserts the estimated dollar cost of LLM usage per step. Cost is derived from token counts and a model price table.

## Goals
- Estimate cost (USD) for each step from recorded token metrics
- Allow thresholds (`max_cost_usd`) to fail expensive runs
- Support multiple models within a single step

## Acceptance Criteria
1. New evaluator type `llm_cost`
2. Price table configurable via env or default map
3. Evaluator passes if total step cost <= `max_cost_usd`
4. Detailed message with model breakdown and total

## Schema Changes
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`:

```ts
const LlmCostEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('llm_cost'),
  max_cost_usd: z.number(),
});
```

## Pricing Source
Add a small utility `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/pricing.ts`:

```ts
export type ModelPricing = {
  inputPer1K: number;    // USD per 1000 input tokens
  outputPer1K: number;   // USD per 1000 output tokens
};

export const DEFAULT_MODEL_PRICING: Record<string, ModelPricing> = {
  TEXT_SMALL: { inputPer1K: 0.15, outputPer1K: 0.60 },
  TEXT_LARGE: { inputPer1K: 0.50, outputPer1K: 1.50 },
  OBJECT_SMALL: { inputPer1K: 0.50, outputPer1K: 1.50 },
};

export function getPricing(modelType: string, overrides?: Record<string, ModelPricing>): ModelPricing | null {
  const table = overrides ?? DEFAULT_MODEL_PRICING;
  return table[modelType] ?? null;
}
```

Allow overrides via `SCENARIO_MODEL_PRICING` env (JSON string) in a follow-up.

## Evaluation Implementation
Add to `EvaluationEngine`:

```ts
class LlmCostEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params: EvaluationSchema, runResult: ExecutionResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'llm_cost') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const llm = runResult.metrics?.llm ?? [];
    if (!llm.length) return { success: false, message: 'No LLM metrics found for cost calculation' };

    const pricingOverrides = process.env.SCENARIO_MODEL_PRICING ? JSON.parse(process.env.SCENARIO_MODEL_PRICING) : undefined;
    let total = 0;
    for (const m of llm) {
      const pricing = getPricing(m.modelType, pricingOverrides);
      if (!pricing) continue;
      const inTok = m.promptTokens ?? 0;
      const outTok = m.completionTokens ?? 0;
      total += (inTok / 1000) * pricing.inputPer1K + (outTok / 1000) * pricing.outputPer1K;
    }

    const success = total <= params.max_cost_usd;
    return { success, message: `Estimated cost: $${total.toFixed(4)} (limit $${params.max_cost_usd.toFixed(4)})` };
  }
}
```

Register:

```ts
this.register('llm_cost', new LlmCostEvaluator());
```

## Example Usage

```yaml
evaluations:
  - type: llm_cost
    max_cost_usd: 0.05
```

## Tests
- Unit: price math with multiple model records
- Integration: with token_count metrics present and absent

## Notes
This builds on the Token Count evaluator and shared metrics capture. It complements mocking enhancements described in [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726).


24:Tf93,# feat(scenarios): Add Token Count Evaluator

Links: [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726)

## Summary
Add an evaluator to assert on input/output/total token counts for LLM calls used during a scenario step. This establishes cost-awareness and guardrails on prompt size and verbosity.

## Goals
- Track token counts for LLM interactions triggered by a step
- Assert against `max_input_tokens`, `max_output_tokens`, optional `max_total_tokens`
- Work across providers through `AgentRuntime.useModel` hooks

## Acceptance Criteria
1. New evaluator type `token_count`
2. Instrumentation captures prompt/completion token counts per step
3. Evaluator passes if counts are within configured maxima
4. Messages include breakdown and model type(s)

## Schema Changes
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`:

```ts
const TokenCountEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('token_count'),
  max_input_tokens: z.number().optional(),
  max_output_tokens: z.number().optional(),
  max_total_tokens: z.number().optional(),
});

// Add to EvaluationSchema union
```

## Runtime Integration
We will attach a per-step metrics accumulator that the evaluators can read via `ExecutionResult` metadata. Minimal, provider-agnostic approach:

### ExecutionResult extension
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/providers.ts`:

```ts
export interface ExecutionResult {
  // ...existing
  metrics?: {
    llm?: Array<{
      modelType: string;
      promptTokens?: number;
      completionTokens?: number;
      totalTokens?: number;
      provider?: string;
    }>;
  };
}
```

### Capturing tokens
Add a light hook layer around `AgentRuntime.useModel` during scenario runs:

- In `runtime-factory.ts`, wrap the runtime instance for scenarios with a proxy that:
  - Intercepts `useModel`
  - If params include a prompt or known text fields, call tokenizer via `ModelType.TEXT_TOKENIZER_ENCODE` to estimate prompt tokens
  - After result, estimate output tokens similarly
  - Push a record into `currentStepMetrics.llm[]`

Note: If a tokenizer is not available, fall back to heuristic length/4 estimate. Keep it optional so normal runs are unaffected.

## Evaluation Implementation
Add to `EvaluationEngine`:

```ts
class TokenCountEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params: EvaluationSchema, runResult: ExecutionResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'token_count') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const llm = runResult.metrics?.llm ?? [];
    if (!llm.length) {
      return { success: false, message: 'No LLM token metrics found for this step' };
    }
    const totals = llm.reduce((acc, m) => ({
      prompt: acc.prompt + (m.promptTokens ?? 0),
      completion: acc.completion + (m.completionTokens ?? 0),
      total: acc.total + (m.totalTokens ?? ((m.promptTokens ?? 0) + (m.completionTokens ?? 0))),
    }), { prompt: 0, completion: 0, total: 0 });

    const tooManyInput = params.max_input_tokens != null && totals.prompt > params.max_input_tokens;
    const tooManyOutput = params.max_output_tokens != null && totals.completion > params.max_output_tokens;
    const tooManyTotal = params.max_total_tokens != null && totals.total > params.max_total_tokens;

    const success = !(tooManyInput || tooManyOutput || tooManyTotal);
    return {
      success,
      message: `Tokens — input:${totals.prompt}, output:${totals.completion}, total:${totals.total}`,
    };
  }
}
```

Register in constructor:

```ts
this.register('token_count', new TokenCountEvaluator());
```

## Example Usage

```yaml
evaluations:
  - type: token_count
    max_input_tokens: 2000
    max_output_tokens: 1000
    max_total_tokens: 2500
```

## Tests
- Unit: evaluator aggregation logic
- Integration: scenario with a small prompt + enforced limits; tokenizer present and absent

## Notes
- This is complementary to a Cost evaluator which can derive cost from token metrics and model price tables.


25:Te7b,# feat(scenarios): Add Execution Time Evaluator

Links: [Issue #5726](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/issues/5726)

## Summary
Add an evaluator that measures per-step execution duration and asserts against bounds (max/min/target). This is foundational for performance benchmarking and simple SLAs.

## Goals
- Measure execution duration for each `run` step
- Expose timing data to evaluations
- Evaluate against `max_duration_ms`, optional `min_duration_ms`, and optional `target_duration_ms`

## Acceptance Criteria
1. New evaluator type `execution_time` supported by scenarios
2. Duration recorded for both Local and E2B environments
3. Evaluator passes when duration <= `max_duration_ms` (and >= `min_duration_ms` if provided)
4. Detailed message includes actual duration
5. Unit tests and example scenario added

## Schema Changes
Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts` to add:

```ts
const ExecutionTimeEvaluationSchema = BaseEvaluationSchema.extend({
  type: z.literal('execution_time'),
  max_duration_ms: z.number(),
  min_duration_ms: z.number().optional(),
  target_duration_ms: z.number().optional(),
});

// Add to union
// ... EvaluationSchema = z.discriminatedUnion('type', [
//   ...,
//   ExecutionTimeEvaluationSchema,
// ]);
```

## Runtime/Providers Changes
Add timing capture to `ExecutionResult` and both environment providers.

Edit `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/providers.ts`:

```ts
export interface ExecutionResult {
  exitCode: number;
  stdout: string;
  stderr: string;
  files: Record<string, string>;
  startedAtMs?: number; // epoch ms
  endedAtMs?: number;   // epoch ms
  durationMs?: number;  // derived convenience
}
```

Instrument `LocalEnvironmentProvider.run` and `E2BEnvironmentProvider.run` to set `startedAtMs`, `endedAtMs`, `durationMs` around each step execution.

## EvaluationEngine Changes
Add a new evaluator implementation:

```ts
// type: execution_time
class ExecutionTimeEvaluator implements Evaluator {
  async evaluate(params: EvaluationSchema, runResult: ExecutionResult): Promise<EvaluationResult> {
    if (params.type !== 'execution_time') throw new Error('Mismatched evaluator');
    const duration = runResult.durationMs ?? ((runResult as any).endedAtMs - (runResult as any).startedAtMs);
    if (duration == null || Number.isNaN(duration)) {
      return { success: false, message: 'No timing information available for this step' };
    }
    const tooSlow = duration > params.max_duration_ms;
    const tooFast = params.min_duration_ms != null && duration < params.min_duration_ms;
    const success = !tooSlow && !tooFast;
    return {
      success,
      message: `Execution time ${duration}ms (min=${params.min_duration_ms ?? '-'}, target=${params.target_duration_ms ?? '-'}, max=${params.max_duration_ms})`,
    };
  }
}
```

Register in `EvaluationEngine` constructor:

```ts
this.register('execution_time', new ExecutionTimeEvaluator());
```

## Example Scenario Snippet

```yaml
evaluations:
  - type: execution_time
    max_duration_ms: 5000
    min_duration_ms: 50
    target_duration_ms: 1000
```

## Tests
- Unit tests for evaluator with mocked `ExecutionResult` values
- Integration tests for Local/E2B providers to ensure timing fields are present

## Risks and Mitigations
- Clock skew: use single-process monotonic where feasible (Date.now is acceptable low-lift). For E2B, measure from the orchestrator around the call boundary.
- Missing timing fields: evaluator should fail gracefully with a clear message.

## Rollout
1. Schema + providers timing fields
2. Evaluator registration and implementation
3. Tests and example docs
4. Follow-ups: percentile/throughput aggregations in separate tickets


26:Tabb,This service offers a *deterministic* OpenAI-compliant LLM inference endpoint that can be used via multiple clients. A couple of key points:

* By OpenAI compliancy we specifically mean the messages-based Chat Completions API: [https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/chat/create](https://platform.openai.com/docs/api-reference/chat/create),
* By “deterministic” we specifically mean that one request (prompt, parameters, etc) provided to the API multiple times will produce the **same** output.
  * For eg. setting a different seed but otherwise keeping the request the same will produce a different output.

## curl

```
curl -s -X POST http://192.222.58.254:8001/v1/chat/completions \
-H "Content-Type: application/json" \
-d '{
  "model": "gemma-3-27b-it",
  "max_completion_tokens": 120,
  "seed": 42,
  "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Write a story about programming"}]
}' | jq
```

### Streaming response

```
curl -s -X POST http://192.222.58.254:8001/v1/chat/completions \
-H "Content-Type: application/json" \
-d '{
  "model": "gemma-3-27b-it",
  "max_completion_tokens": 120,
  "seed": 42,
  "stream": true,
  "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "Write a story about programming"}]
}'
```

## Vercel AI SDK

[https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/introduction](https://ai-sdk.dev/docs/introduction)

```
import { generateText } from 'ai';
import { createOpenAI } from '@ai-sdk/openai';

const config = {
  baseURL: 'http://192.222.58.254:8001/v1',
  apiKey: 'sk-dummy-custom-key',
  model: 'gemma-3-27b-it'
};

// Create a custom OpenAI client instance
const customOpenAI = createOpenAI({
  baseURL: config.baseURL,
  apiKey: config.apiKey,
});

// Create the model instance using the custom client
const model = customOpenAI(config.model);

async function chatCompletion() {
  console.log('\n💬 Testing chat completion...');
  
  try {
    const result = await generateText({
      model: model,
      messages: [
        { role: 'system', content: 'You are a helpful assistant.' },
        { role: 'user', content: 'What are the benefits of using local AI models?' }
      ],
      seed: 42
    });

    console.log('✅ Chat response:');
    console.log(result.text);
  } catch (error) {
    console.error('❌ Error with chat completion:');
    console.error(error.message);
  }
}

// Main execution
async function main() {
  console.log('EigenAI usage via Vercel AI SDK\n');
  console.log('=' + '='.repeat(49));
  
  console.log(`\n🔧 Configuration:`);
  console.log(`   Service: EigenAI`);
  console.log(`   Base URL: ${config.baseURL}`);
  console.log(`   Model: ${config.model}`);
  console.log(`   API Key: ${config.apiKey.substring(0, 10)}...`);
  
  await chatCompletion();
}

main().catch(console.error);
```27:T986,**Goal**  
Create a repeatable, apples-to-apples benchmark suite that measures **accuracy, latency, and cost** for representative Eliza use cases. Results drive merit-based architectural decisions for v3 (and guardrails for future changes).

**Why now**  
We need baselines on v2 and the same tests running on v3 candidates to validate improvements in **DevEx, production readiness, tool calling, streaming, and opinionated defaults**.

## Scope

1. **Unified runner** that can execute the same test specs against:
   * **v2** (current runtime; actions/providers/event bus as implemented)
   * **v3** (tool loop prototype; declarative workflows; Drizzle/Redis stack)
2. **Scenario set** that reflects real user value and stress points:
   * **Tool calling & workflows** (multi-step, branching, error handling)
   * **Streaming path** (token + tool streaming)
   * **Multilingual** (detect → translate → run → translate-back)
   * **RAG / vector** (retrieve-then-reason with pluggable vectors)
   * **Service events** (webhook/listener → agent response)
   * **DevEx** proxy (boot → first streamed token; commands executed)
   * **Interop** (OpenAI-compatible tool calls; MCP remote tool)
   * **Deterministic workflows** (replay under fixed seeds)
3. **Telemetry + dataset loop**
   * Trajectory logging → dataset building → evals (LLM-as-judge + rubric)

## Metric Definitions (source of truth)

**Latency**

* **TTFT**: time-to-first-token (ms)
* **TTFR**: time-to-final-response (ms)
* **TTS1**: time-to-first tool-call span (ms)
* **Per-step latency**: tool span durations (p50/p95)
* **Tokens/sec**: output token throughput during streaming

**Cost**

* **Prompt tokens / Completion tokens**
* **Total tokens / scenario**
* **Approx $** (using model price sheet plugged into the runner; version-pinned)

**Accuracy / Reliability**

* **Task success** (boolean via structured checks or rubric)
* **Tool-call correctness** (valid schema, correct args)
* **Groundedness** (RAG: citation presence + answer-supports-evidence score)
* **Hallucination rate** (rubric-based)
* **Replay determinism** (variance across n=5 runs with fixed seed)
* **Error rate** (non-200, exception, timeout, tool failure)

**DevEx proxies**

* **Commands to run** (from template scaffold to first streamed token)
* **Cold start to TTFT** (scripted create→dev→curl path)
* **Config surface** (count of required fields; measured from template)28:T1d0f,Free Tier only allows 1 endpoint interaction / 15 mins

   class VeniceModelProvider implements ModelProvider {
     async generateText(params: GenerateTextParams) {
       const response = await fetch("https://api.venice.ai/v1/chat/completions", {
         method: "POST",
         headers: {
           "Authorization": `Bearer ${process.env.VENICE_API_KEY}`,
           "Content-Type": "application/json"
         },
         body: JSON.stringify({
           model: params.model,
           messages: params.messages,
           temperature: params.temperature
         })
       });

       const data = await response.json();
       return data.choices[0].message.content;

# Risks

Low. This PR fixes a display bug and adds error handling for invalid input, improving robustness without introducing new functionality.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

*   Corrects the `clearAgentMemories` command to use `result?.deletedCount` instead of `result?.deleted` to accurately display the number of cleared memories.
*   Adds robust error handling for `asUUID(resolvedAgentId)` calls in `removeAgent`, `clearAgentMemories`, and `setAgentConfig` commands. This prevents unhandled errors when an invalid agent ID format (non-UUID) is provided.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

The `clearAgentMemories` command was incorrectly displaying '0 memories cleared' because it expected a `deleted` property from the API response, while the API returns `deletedCount`. Additionally, the `removeAgent`, `clearAgentMemories`, and `setAgentConfig` commands lacked proper error handling for invalid UUIDs passed to `asUUID`, which could lead to unhandled exceptions.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cli/src/commands/agent/actions/crud.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

*   **Verify `clearAgentMemories` count display**:
    1.  Ensure an agent has some memories (e.g., by interacting with it).
    2.  Run `npm run cli agent clear-memories --name <agent-name>` (or by UUID/index).
    3.  Verify the output correctly displays the number of cleared memories (e.g., "Successfully cleared X memories...").
*   **Verify `asUUID` error handling**:
    1.  Run `npm run cli agent remove --name invalid-uuid-format`.
    2.  Verify an error message like "Invalid agent ID format: invalid-uuid-format. Please provide a valid UUID, agent name, or index." is displayed.
    3.  Repeat steps 1 and 2 for `npm run cli agent clear-memories --name invalid-uuid-format`.
    4.  Repeat steps 1 and 2 for `npm run cli agent set --name invalid-uuid-format --config '{ "name": "test" }'`.

# Risks

Low. This PR improves error handling without changing core logic.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR enhances the `getAgent` command by adding robust error handling for UUID conversion. It wraps the `asUUID(resolvedAgentId)` call in a try-catch block, providing a more descriptive error message if the `resolvedAgentId` cannot be converted to a valid UUID.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes (non-breaking change which fixes an issue)
Improvements (misc. changes to existing features)

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

The `getAgent` command's use of `asUUID(resolvedAgentId)` could lead to runtime failures if `resolvedAgentId` (even after being resolved from a name, index, or string ID) is not a valid UUID. While `resolveAgentId` is intended to return a UUID, this change adds a safeguard against potential data inconsistencies or unexpected inputs, providing a clearer, user-friendly error message instead of a generic validation error. This improves the command's resilience.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cli/src/commands/agent/actions/crud.ts` at line 31.

## Detailed testing steps

1.  **Verify existing functionality**:
    *   Create an agent: `eliza agent create --name myagent`
    *   Get the agent by name: `eliza agent get --name myagent` (should succeed)
    *   Get the agent by its UUID (copy from `eliza agent list`): `eliza agent get --id <UUID>` (should succeed)
    *   Get the agent by index: `eliza agent get --index 0` (should succeed)
2.  **Verify new error handling**:
    *   Attempt to get an agent with a clearly invalid, non-UUID string that `resolveAgentId` might theoretically pass through (e.g., `eliza agent get --id "not-a-uuid"`).
    *   Verify that the command now outputs the custom error message: "Invalid agent ID format: not-a-uuid. Please provide a valid UUID, agent name, or index."

# Risks

Low - This change only affects the output format of agent configuration and does not alter core functionality or data.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR restores the previous behavior of excluding the `enabled` field from the agent configuration when saving to a file (using `--output`) or displaying as JSON (using `--json`).

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fixes

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

The `enabled` field was inadvertently included in the agent configuration output, which was a regression from the previous behavior where it was explicitly excluded. This fix ensures consistency with the expected output format.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cli/src/commands/agent/actions/crud.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

1.  Run the agent command with the `--output` flag:
    `your-cli-command agent get --output agent_config.json`
    Verify that `agent_config.json` does *not* contain the `enabled` field.
2.  Run the agent command with the `--json` flag:
    `your-cli-command agent get --json`
    Verify that the JSON output in the console does *not* contain the `enabled` field.

31:T3be3,# Draft Pull Request: Implement Dynamic Prompting (Multi-Turn Conversations) in ElizaOS Scenarios

## 🎯 Overview

This PR implements **Dynamic Prompting** (multi-turn conversations) in ElizaOS scenarios, enabling sophisticated testing of agent behavior through extended conversations where an LLM simulates realistic user responses. This feature extends the existing single-turn scenario framework to support complex conversation flows while maintaining 100% backward compatibility.

**Ticket**: [ELIZA-669](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELIZA-669/implement-dynamic-prompting-multi-turn-conversations-in)

## 🚀 Key Features

### ✨ Multi-Turn Conversation Support
- **LLM User Simulator**: Generates realistic, persona-driven user responses
- **Conversation Orchestration**: Manages multi-turn execution with termination logic
- **Turn-Level Evaluations**: Real-time assessment during conversation flow
- **Advanced Evaluators**: New conversation-specific evaluation types

### 🔄 Backward Compatibility
- **100% Compatible**: All existing single-turn scenarios work unchanged
- **Gradual Adoption**: Teams can enhance existing scenarios incrementally
- **No Breaking Changes**: Existing APIs and CLI commands remain unchanged

### 🧠 Intelligent Conversation Management
- **Termination Conditions**: Multiple strategies for ending conversations early
- **Context Retention**: Agent memory testing across conversation turns
- **Emotional Intelligence**: Handling complex user personas and emotional states
- **Performance Optimization**: Timeout mechanisms and resource management

## 📁 Files Changed

### New Files
```
packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/
├── conversation-types.ts          # TypeScript interfaces for conversation components
├── UserSimulator.ts              # LLM-based user response generation
├── ConversationManager.ts        # Multi-turn conversation orchestration
├── ConversationEvaluators.ts    # New conversation-specific evaluators
└── __tests__/
    ├── UserSimulator.test.ts     # Unit tests for user simulator
    ├── ConversationManager.test.ts # Unit tests for conversation manager
    ├── ConversationEvaluators.test.ts # Unit tests for new evaluators
    ├── schema-conversation.test.ts # Schema validation tests
    └── integration/
        ├── conversation-flow.test.ts # Integration tests
        └── backward-compatibility.test.ts # Compatibility tests
```

### Modified Files
```
packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/
├── schema.ts                     # Extended with conversation schemas
├── LocalEnvironmentProvider.ts   # Added conversation support
├── E2BEnvironmentProvider.ts     # Added conversation support
└── EvaluationEngine.ts           # Registered new evaluators
```

### New Example Files
```
packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/examples/
├── basic-conversation.yaml       # Basic multi-turn conversation
├── emotional-intelligence.yaml   # Complex persona testing
├── technical-support.yaml        # Troubleshooting conversation
└── knowledge-transfer.yaml       # Educational conversation
```

## 🔧 Technical Implementation

### Schema Extensions
- **ConversationConfigSchema**: Defines conversation configuration with user simulator settings
- **New Evaluation Types**: `conversation_length`, `conversation_flow`, `user_satisfaction`, `context_retention`
- **Backward Compatible**: Optional `conversation` field in existing `RunStepSchema`

### User Simulator
- **Persona-Driven**: Configurable personality, objectives, constraints, and knowledge level
- **Context-Aware**: Builds prompts based on conversation history and agent responses
- **Realistic Generation**: LLM-based response generation with behavioral constraints

### Conversation Manager
- **Multi-Turn Orchestration**: Manages conversation flow and turn execution
- **Termination Logic**: Intelligent conversation ending based on multiple conditions
- **Evaluation Integration**: Supports both turn-level and final evaluations
- **Error Handling**: Graceful handling of failures and timeouts

### New Evaluators
- **ConversationLengthEvaluator**: Validates optimal conversation duration
- **ConversationFlowEvaluator**: Detects required conversation patterns
- **UserSatisfactionEvaluator**: Measures user satisfaction through multiple methods
- **ContextRetentionEvaluator**: Verifies agent memory across conversation turns

## 📋 Configuration Examples

### Basic Multi-Turn Conversation
```yaml
run:
  - input: "Hi, I need help with something"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 4
      user_simulator:
        persona: "polite customer with a billing question"
        objective: "find out why charged twice this month"
        temperature: 0.6
      final_evaluations:
        - type: "llm_judge"
          prompt: "Did the agent successfully help resolve the billing issue?"
          expected: "yes"
```

### Advanced Emotional Intelligence Testing
```yaml
run:
  - input: "This is ridiculous! Your product doesn't work!"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 6
      user_simulator:
        persona: "angry customer who had bad experience"
        objective: "vent frustration but eventually want help"
        style: "initially hostile, gradually becomes cooperative if handled well"
        constraints:
          - "Start with complaints and criticism"
          - "Become more cooperative if agent shows empathy"
      termination_conditions:
        - type: "user_expresses_satisfaction"
        - type: "agent_escalates_to_human"
      final_evaluations:
        - type: "user_satisfaction"
          satisfaction_threshold: 0.6
        - type: "conversation_flow"
          required_patterns: ["empathy_then_solution", "clarification_cycle"]
```

## 🧪 Testing Strategy

### Unit Tests
- **UserSimulator**: Tests persona-driven response generation and constraint handling
- **ConversationManager**: Tests turn execution, termination logic, and error handling
- **ConversationEvaluators**: Tests all new evaluation types with various scenarios
- **Schema Validation**: Tests conversation schema validation and backward compatibility

### Integration Tests
- **Conversation Flow**: End-to-end conversation execution with realistic scenarios
- **Provider Integration**: Tests both local and cloud environment providers
- **Evaluation Integration**: Tests new evaluators with existing evaluation engine
- **Backward Compatibility**: Ensures existing scenarios work unchanged

### Performance Tests
- **Resource Usage**: Memory and CPU usage validation for long conversations
- **LLM API Optimization**: Token usage and rate limiting verification
- **Timeout Handling**: Tests timeout mechanisms and graceful degradation

## 📊 Performance Impact

### Resource Usage
- **Memory**: Additional ~50MB per conversation (configurable limits)
- **CPU**: Minimal impact, primarily during LLM API calls
- **Network**: Additional LLM API calls for user simulation (optimized with caching)

### Execution Time
- **Single-Turn Scenarios**: No impact (unchanged execution path)
- **Multi-Turn Scenarios**: ~30-60 seconds per turn (configurable timeouts)
- **Matrix Testing**: Parallel execution with rate limiting

### LLM API Usage
- **User Simulation**: ~200 tokens per turn (configurable)
- **Evaluation**: ~100 tokens per evaluation (existing pattern)
- **Rate Limiting**: Built-in throttling and retry logic

## 🔒 Security & Reliability

### Error Handling
- **LLM API Failures**: Retry logic with exponential backoff
- **Timeout Mechanisms**: Configurable timeouts per turn and total conversation
- **Graceful Degradation**: Fallback to simpler simulation strategies
- **Resource Limits**: Memory and execution time limits to prevent runaway conversations

### Data Privacy
- **No Persistent Storage**: Conversation data not stored beyond execution
- **Transcript Export**: Optional full conversation export for debugging
- **Logging Control**: Configurable debug logging levels

## 🚦 Migration Guide

### For Existing Scenarios
**No changes required!** All existing scenarios continue to work unchanged:

```yaml
# Existing scenario - works exactly as before
run:
  - input: "Hello agent"
    evaluations:
      - type: "string_contains"
        value: "hello"
```

### For Enhanced Scenarios
Gradually add conversation features to existing scenarios:

```yaml
# Enhanced scenario - adds conversation while keeping existing evaluations
run:
  - input: "Hello agent"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 2
      user_simulator:
        persona: "friendly user"
        objective: "have brief chat"
    evaluations:  # Keep existing evaluations for compatibility
      - type: "string_contains"
        value: "hello"
```

### For New Conversation-First Scenarios
Create scenarios designed for multi-turn testing:

```yaml
# New conversation-first scenario
run:
  - input: "I have a complex problem"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 8
      user_simulator:
        persona: "confused user with technical issue"
        objective: "get step-by-step help"
      final_evaluations:
        - type: "user_satisfaction"
          satisfaction_threshold: 0.7
        - type: "conversation_length"
          optimal_turns: 5
```

## 📈 Future Enhancements

### Planned Features
- **Advanced Personas**: More sophisticated user personality modeling
- **Multi-Agent Conversations**: Support for multiple agents in conversation
- **Emotional Intelligence**: Enhanced emotional state tracking and response
- **Conversation Analytics**: Advanced conversation quality metrics
- **Custom Evaluators**: Framework for custom conversation evaluators

### Performance Optimizations
- **Response Caching**: Cache common user simulation responses
- **Parallel Processing**: Concurrent evaluation execution
- **Streaming Transcripts**: Real-time conversation monitoring
- **Resource Pooling**: Shared LLM connection pools

## ✅ Acceptance Criteria

### Functional Requirements

33:Ta8f,## Summary
Adds a universal AI Gateway plugin that provides access to 100+ AI models through Vercel AI Gateway and other OpenAI-compatible gateways.

## Features
- 🚀 **100+ AI Models** - OpenAI, Anthropic, Google, Meta, Mistral, and more through unified gateways
- 🔄 **Universal Gateway Support** - Works with Vercel AI Gateway, OpenRouter, and any OpenAI-compatible endpoint
- 💾 **Response Caching** - Built-in LRU cache for cost optimization
- 📊 **Built-in Telemetry** - Track usage and performance metrics
- ⚡ **High Performance** - Automatic retry logic and connection pooling
- 🎯 **Multiple Actions** - Text generation, image generation, embeddings, and model listing

## Highlights

### Vercel AI Gateway Integration
- Optimized for Vercel's AI Gateway with proper URL format (`https://ai-gateway.vercel.sh/v1`)
- Supports Vercel's colon-based model naming convention (e.g., `openai:gpt-4o`)
- Compatible with Vercel's app attribution headers for analytics

### Flexible Provider Support
- Works with any OpenAI-compatible API endpoint
- Automatic model format detection (colon vs slash separators)
- Seamless switching between providers with minimal configuration

### Enterprise Features
- Response caching with configurable TTL
- Automatic retry logic with exponential backoff
- OIDC authentication support for enterprise deployments
- Comprehensive error handling and fallback mechanisms

## Installation

The plugin is already published to npm for immediate use:
```bash
npm install @promptordie/plugin-aigateway
```

After merge, it will be available as:
```bash
npm install @elizaos/plugin-aigateway
```

## Configuration

```env
# Required
AIGATEWAY_API_KEY=your_api_key_here

# Optional (defaults shown)
AIGATEWAY_BASE_URL=https://ai-gateway.vercel.sh/v1
AIGATEWAY_DEFAULT_MODEL=openai:gpt-4o-mini
AIGATEWAY_LARGE_MODEL=openai:gpt-4o
AIGATEWAY_EMBEDDING_MODEL=openai:text-embedding-3-small
AIGATEWAY_CACHE_TTL=300
AIGATEWAY_MAX_RETRIES=3
```

## Usage

```typescript
import aiGatewayPlugin from '@elizaos/plugin-aigateway';

const character = {
    name: 'MyAgent',
    plugins: [aiGatewayPlugin],
    settings: {
        AIGATEWAY_API_KEY: 'your-api-key'
    }
};
```

## Related Links
- NPM Package: https://www.npmjs.com/package/@promptordie/plugin-aigateway
- Vercel AI Gateway Docs: https://vercel.com/docs/ai-gateway34:Tca5,## Problem

The local development server was crashing with a React rendering error:
```
Objects are not valid as a React child (found: object with keys {name, email})
```

This occurred because some package.json files in plugin repositories have the `author` field as an object with `name` and `email` properties, rather than a simple string. React cannot render objects directly as children, causing the application to crash.

## Root Cause

In the plugin registry app, when fetching package.json files from GitHub repositories, the code was directly using `content.author` without checking if it was an object or string. When package.json contained:
```json
{
  "author": {
    "name": "John Doe",
    "email": "john@example.com"
  }
}
```

Instead of:
```json
{
  "author": "John Doe"
}
```

React would try to render the object directly, causing the error.

## Solution

### 1. Author Field Handling
- Added proper type checking for the `author` field
- Extract `name` or `email` from author objects gracefully
- Fallback to 'unknown' if neither is available

### 2. Enhanced Error Handling
- Improved network error handling for package.json fetches
- Better error boundaries for missing or malformed data
- More robust fallback mechanisms

### 3. TypeScript Improvements
- Fixed type safety for error state (`string | null` instead of `null`)
- Added proper fallback values for missing fields

### 4. Code Structure Improvements
- Refactored nested try-catch blocks for better readability
- Consolidated content handling logic
- Added comprehensive error logging

## Changes Made

```typescript
// Before
author: content.author,

// After
author: typeof content.author === 'object' 
  ? content.author?.name || content.author?.email || 'unknown'
  : content.author || 'unknown',
```

## Testing

- ✅ Local development server runs without React rendering errors
- ✅ Plugin registry loads successfully with mixed author field types
- ✅ Graceful handling of 404s for missing package.json files
- ✅ Proper fallbacks for missing or malformed data
- ✅ TypeScript compilation without errors

## Impact

- Fixes critical React rendering crash in local development
- Improves robustness of plugin data fetching
- Better user experience with graceful error handling
- Maintains backward compatibility with existing string-based authors

This fix ensures the plugin registry can handle the variety of package.json formats found in the ElizaOS plugin ecosystem without crashing.

### Overview
This PR significantly enhances the sessions API with comprehensive timeout management, auto-renewal capabilities, and robust error handling. The changes transform the basic session management into an enterprise-ready system with configurable lifecycles, warning states, and graceful cleanup mechanisms.

### Key Improvements

#### 🔧 **Advanced Timeout Configuration System**
**Before:** Simple fixed timeout (`SESSION_TIMEOUT_MS`)
**After:** Multi-layered timeout configuration with:
- Configurable timeout minutes (5-1440 min range)
- Auto-renewal capabilities
- Maximum session duration limits
- Warning threshold notifications
- Agent-specific timeout settings
- Session-specific overrides

#### 🛡️ **Robust Error Handling**
**Before:** Basic `errorResponse` function with generic error messages
**After:** Comprehensive error system with:
- 11 custom error classes (`SessionNotFoundError`, `SessionExpiredError`, etc.)
- Detailed error context and metadata
- Centralized error middleware
- Type-safe error handling throughout

#### ♻️ **Session Lifecycle Management**
**New Features:**
- **Auto-renewal**: Sessions automatically extend on activity
- **Manual renewal**: `/sessions/:id/renew` endpoint
- **Heartbeat support**: `/sessions/:id/heartbeat` for keep-alive
- **Warning states**: Proactive expiration notifications
- **Renewal tracking**: Monitor renewal counts and patterns
- **Graceful expiration**: Proper cleanup of expired sessions

#### 🎯 **Type Safety Enhancements**
**Added:**
- Type guards for all request/response objects
- `isValidSession()`, `isCreateSessionRequest()`, `isSendMessageRequest()`
- `isValidTimeoutConfig()` for configuration validation
- Proper TypeScript interfaces for all data structures

#### 🔌 **New API Endpoints**
```
POST   /sessions/:id/renew      - Manually renew a session
POST   /sessions/:id/heartbeat  - Keep session alive
PATCH  /sessions/:id/timeout    - Update timeout configuration
```

#### 📊 **Enhanced Health Monitoring**
**Before:** Basic session count
**After:** Detailed health metrics including:
- Active vs expired sessions
- Sessions expiring soon
- Invalid session detection
- Server uptime tracking

#### 🧹 **Memory Leak Prevention**
**New:**
- `SessionRouter` interface with cleanup method
- Proper interval management with `activeCleanupIntervals` Set
- Process handler registration tracking
- Resource cleanup on router destruction

#### 📈 **Improved Pagination**
**Enhanced:**
- Better cursor-based pagination with proper `before`/`after` handling
- Cursor information in response for easier client implementation
- Proper handling of edge cases in date range queries

### Breaking Changes
None - All existing endpoints maintain backward compatibility while adding optional new features.

### Migration Guide
Existing clients will continue to work without changes. To leverage new features:

1. **Enable auto-renewal**: Include `timeoutConfig: { autoRenew: true }` in session creation
2. **Set custom timeouts**: Add `timeoutConfig: { timeoutMinutes: 60 }` 
3. **Monitor expiration**: Check `isNearExpiration` in responses
4. **Use heartbeats**: Send periodic POST to `/sessions/:id/heartbeat`

### Configuration
New environment variables (all optional):
```env
SESSION_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_MINUTES=30      # Default: 30
SESSION_MIN_TIMEOUT_MINUTES=5           # Default: 5  
SESSION_MAX_TIMEOUT_MINUTES=1440        # Default: 1440 (24h)
SESSION_MAX_DURATION_MINUTES=720        # Default: 720 (12h)
SESSION_WARNING_THRESHOLD_MINUTES=5     # Default: 5
SESSION_CLEANUP_INTERVAL_MINUTES=5      # Default: 5
CLEAR_SESSIONS_ON_SHUTDOWN=false        # Default: false
```

### Technical Details

#### Session State Structure
```typescript
interface Session {
  // ... existing fields ...
  expiresAt: Date;              // NEW: Calculated expiration time
  timeoutConfig: {              // NEW: Comprehensive timeout settings
    timeoutMinutes?: number;
    autoRenew?: boolean;
    maxDurationMinutes?: number;
    warningThresholdMinutes?: number;
  };
  renewalCount: number;         // NEW: Track renewal history
  warningState?: {              // NEW: Warning notification state
    sent: boolean;
    sentAt: Date;
  };
}
```

  - `InvalidUuidError`
  - `MissingFieldsError`
  - `InvalidContentError`
  - `InvalidMetadataError`
  - `InvalidPaginationError`
  - `InvalidTimeoutConfigError`
  - `AgentNotFoundError`

// Fixed logger bindings causing "Cannot read properties of undefined"

  warn: originalConsole.warn.bind(originalConsole),

// Example test structure
{
  name: 'should_execute_quick_action',
  fn: async (runtime) => {
    const handler = runtime.actions.get('QUICK_ACTION')?.handler;
    const result = await handler(runtime, mockMessage, mockState, {}, mockCallback);

  }

## Technical Details

### E2E Test Integration Pattern
```typescript
// Plugin exports tests directly
export const myPlugin: Plugin = {
  name: 'my-plugin',
  actions: [...],
  providers: [...],
  services: [...],

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->3a:Tdd0,# Relates to

Performance improvement for message processing latency - embeddings were blocking runtime for 500ms+ per message

# Risks

**Low risk** - The change is backward compatible and includes fallback behavior. Main risks:
- Memory usage could increase if embedding queue grows large (mitigated by priority queue and processing limits)
- Embeddings may be generated slightly later than before (acceptable trade-off for faster message responses)

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR implements asynchronous embedding generation to prevent blocking the message processing pipeline. Previously, generating embeddings for messages would block the runtime for 500ms+ per API call, delaying message responses. Now embeddings are queued and processed in the background by a dedicated service.

Key changes:
- Added `queueEmbeddingGeneration` method to `AgentRuntime` that emits events instead of blocking
- Created `EmbeddingGenerationService` that listens for `EMBEDDING_QUEUED` events and processes them asynchronously
- Implemented a priority queue system (high/normal/low) for embedding generation
- Added comprehensive test coverage for both the runtime method and service

## What kind of change is this?

**Features** (non-breaking change which adds functionality) + **Improvements** (performance enhancement to existing features)

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes require a change to the project documentation.
- [ ] Need to document the new `queueEmbeddingGeneration` method in runtime API docs
- [ ] Need to document the `EmbeddingGenerationService` in plugin documentation

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Review `packages/core/src/runtime.ts` - see the new `queueEmbeddingGeneration` method (lines 2024-2055)
2. Review `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/services/embedding.ts` - the new service implementation
3. Check the integration in `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/index.ts` where we replaced `addEmbeddingToMemory` calls with `queueEmbeddingGeneration`
4. Review test coverage in:
   - `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/__tests__/embedding-service.test.ts`
   - `packages/core/src/__tests__/runtime-embedding.test.ts`

## Detailed testing steps

### Automated Tests
```bash
# Run core tests
cd packages/core && bun run test

# Run bootstrap plugin tests  
cd packages/plugin-bootstrap && bun run test
```

### Manual Testing
1. Start an agent with the bootstrap plugin
2. Send a message to the agent
3. Observe that:
   - The agent responds quickly (no 500ms delay)
   - Check logs for "Queueing embedding generation" and "Processing embedding for memory"
   - Verify embeddings are eventually generated (check database or memory state)

### Performance Verification
The tests include a specific test case demonstrating non-blocking behavior:
- `"should not block runtime while generating embeddings"` test verifies that embedding generation happens asynchronously
- Response times should be significantly faster (500ms+ improvement per message)

## Before/After Performance

**Before:** Message → Create Memory → Generate Embedding (500ms+) → Process Actions → Respond  
**After:** Message → Create Memory → Queue Embedding (instant) → Process Actions → Respond | (Embedding generated in background)

# Deploy Notes

No deployment changes required. The service is automatically registered when the bootstrap plugin is loaded.

### Overview
This PR introduces a comprehensive refactoring of the logger module to support both browser and Node.js environments while maintaining backward compatibility and improving overall architecture.

### Key Changes

#### 1. **Cross-Platform Support** 🌐
- **Before**: Logger only worked in Node.js environments with direct `pino` imports
- **After**: Universal logger that automatically detects and adapts to the runtime environment (browser/Node.js)
- Added `BrowserLogger` implementation that mimics Pino's API using native `console` methods
- Graceful fallback to `BrowserLogger` when Pino is unavailable

#### 2. **Environment Detection System** 🔍
- Introduced cached environment detection via `createEnvironmentDetector()` factory
- Eliminates redundant environment checks and improves performance
- Provides utility methods: `isNode()`, `isBrowser()`, `hasProcess()`, `getProcessEnv()`

#### 3. **Module Loading Strategy** 📦
- **Dual loading support**:
  - `createLogger()` - Synchronous factory using `require()` (backward compatible)
  - `createLoggerAsync()` - Asynchronous factory using dynamic `import()` (recommended for new code)
- Module caching to prevent redundant imports
- Better error handling for missing dependencies

#### 4. **Improved Memory Management** 💾
- Replaced `InMemoryDestination` class with functional `createInMemoryDestination()` factory
- Configurable max logs via `LOG_MAX_MEMORY_SIZE` env variable (default: 1000)
- More efficient memory buffer management

#### 5. **Enhanced Type Safety** 📝
- Comprehensive TypeScript interfaces:
  - `Logger`, `LogEntry`, `DestinationStream`
  - `BrowserLoggerOptions`, `PinoOptions`
  - `ExtendedPinoLogger` with custom ElizaOS methods
- Better type inference and compile-time checks

#### 6. **Architecture Improvements** 🏗️
- **Modular design**: Clear separation between environment detection, browser logger, Node logger, and configuration
- **Factory pattern**: Consistent use of factory functions for logger creation
- **DRY principle**: Shared core logic via `createLoggerCore()` function
- **Single Responsibility**: Each function has a focused purpose

#### 7. **New Features** ✨
- Test support via `__forceType` binding to force browser/node behavior
- `safeStringify()` for handling circular references
- Better console method detection and fallback logic
- Custom log levels fully integrated in browser logger

### Breaking Changes
None - Full backward compatibility maintained:
- ✅ `createLogger()` still works as before
- ✅ `logger` default export unchanged
- ✅ `elizaLogger` alias preserved
- ✅ All custom log levels (`success`, `progress`, `log`) supported

### Testing Considerations
- New `envDetector` export for testing utilities
- Ability to force logger type for unit tests
- In-memory log buffer accessible for test assertions

### Performance Impact
- ⚡ Cached environment detection reduces repeated checks
- ⚡ Module caching prevents redundant imports
- ⚡ Lazy loading of optional dependencies (pino-pretty)

### Migration Path
No immediate action required. For optimal performance in new code:
```typescript
// Old (still works)
import { createLogger } from './logger';
const logger = createLogger();

// New (recommended for async contexts)
import { createLoggerAsync } from './logger';
const logger = await createLoggerAsync();
```

### File Size
- Old: ~300 lines
- New: ~1000 lines (includes comprehensive browser support, better error handling, and extensive documentation)

### Dependencies
- No new required dependencies
- `pino` and `pino-pretty` remain optional (graceful fallback if unavailable)
