### Problem
The `elizaos create` command was failing when building newly created projects with the error:
```
Cannot find module '../../build-utils' from '/path/to/project/build.ts'
```

This occurred because the template packages (`project-starter`, `project-tee-starter`, `plugin-starter`, `plugin-quick-starter`) contained `build.ts` files that referenced the monorepo's shared `build-utils.ts` using relative paths (`../../build-utils`). When these templates were copied to new project locations by the CLI's create command, those relative paths became invalid.

### Solution
Replaced the build scripts in all template packages with self-contained versions that don't depend on external utilities. Each template now has its own complete build logic using Bun's native build API directly.

### Changes Made
- **`eliza/packages/project-starter/build.ts`** - Self-contained build script for regular projects
- **`eliza/packages/project-tee-starter/build.ts`** - Self-contained build script for TEE projects  
- **`eliza/packages/plugin-starter/build.ts`** - Self-contained build script for plugins
- **`eliza/packages/plugin-quick-starter/build.ts`** - Self-contained build script for quick plugins

1. **Templates are now fully self-contained** - They work correctly regardless of where they're copied to
2. **Simplified build process** - Direct use of Bun's build API without abstraction layers

The Sessions API provides a simplified interface for messaging between users and agents, abstracting away the complexity of servers, channels, and participants.

## Overview

The Sessions API is designed for:
- Simple chat applications
- Discord activities
- Web interfaces
- Mobile apps
- Any client that needs basic user-to-agent messaging

## Key Benefits

1. **Simplified Interface**: No need to manage servers, channels, or participants
2. **Session-based**: Each conversation is a unique session with automatic cleanup
3. **Stateless Clients**: Clients only need to track a session ID
4. **Unified Response Format**: Consistent message format across all endpoints

## API Endpoints

### Create Session
```
POST /api/messaging/sessions
Body: {
  "agentId": "uuid",
  "userId": "uuid",
  "metadata": {} // optional
}
Response: {
  "sessionId": "uuid",
  "agentId": "uuid",
  "userId": "uuid",
  "createdAt": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
  "metadata": {}
}
```

### Send Message
```
POST /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId/messages
Body: {
  "content": "Hello, agent!",
  "attachments": [], // optional
  "metadata": {} // optional
}
Response: {
  "id": "uuid",
  "content": "Hello, agent!",
  "authorId": "user-uuid",
  "createdAt": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
  "metadata": {}
}
```

### Get Messages
```
GET /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId/messages?limit=50&after=timestamp
Response: {
  "messages": [
    {
      "id": "uuid",
      "content": "Hello!",
      "authorId": "uuid",
      "isAgent": true/false,
      "createdAt": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
      "metadata": {
        "thought": "...", // for agent messages
        "actions": [...] // for agent messages
      }
    }
  ],
  "hasMore": true/false
}
```

### Get Session Info
```
GET /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId
Response: {
  "sessionId": "uuid",
  "agentId": "uuid",
  "userId": "uuid",
  "createdAt": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
  "lastActivity": "2024-01-01T00:00:00Z",
  "metadata": {}
}
```

### Delete Session
```
DELETE /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId
Response: {
  "success": true
}
```

## Migration from Simple API

The old Discord-specific simple API can be replaced with the sessions API:

### Old Simple API:
```javascript
// Get agents
GET /api/messaging/simple/agents

// Send message
POST /api/messaging/simple/:agentId/message
Body: { message, sessionId, userId }

// Get messages
GET /api/messaging/simple/:agentId/messages?sessionId=xxx
```

### New Sessions API:
```javascript
// Get agents (use existing endpoint)
GET /api/agents

// Create session first
POST /api/messaging/sessions
Body: { agentId, userId }

// Send message
POST /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId/messages
Body: { content }

// Get messages
GET /api/messaging/sessions/:sessionId/messages
```

## Example Usage

```javascript
// 1. Create a session
const { sessionId } = await fetch('/api/messaging/sessions', {
  method: 'POST',
  headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  body: JSON.stringify({
    agentId: 'agent-uuid',
    userId: 'user-uuid',
    metadata: { platform: 'discord-activity' }
  })
}).then(r => r.json());

// 2. Send a message
await fetch(`/api/messaging/sessions/${sessionId}/messages`, {
  method: 'POST',
  headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },
  body: JSON.stringify({
    content: 'Hello, agent!'
  })
});

// 3. Poll for responses
const pollForResponses = async () => {
  const { messages } = await fetch(
    `/api/messaging/sessions/${sessionId}/messages?after=${lastTimestamp}`
  ).then(r => r.json());
  
  const agentMessages = messages.filter(m => m.isAgent);
  if (agentMessages.length > 0) {
    // Handle agent response
  }
};
```

## Session Lifecycle

1. Sessions are created on-demand when a user starts a conversation
2. Sessions remain active as long as messages are being exchanged
3. Inactive sessions are automatically cleaned up after 30 minutes
4. Clients can explicitly delete sessions when done

## Best Practices

1. **One session per conversation**: Create a new session for each unique conversation
2. **Store session ID**: Clients should persist the session ID for the duration of the conversation
3. **Handle session expiry**: If a session expires, create a new one
4. **Poll efficiently**: Use the `after` parameter to only get new messages
5. **Clean up**: Delete sessions when the conversation ends (optional)3e:T447,- Added `processDocument` method to handle various document types, including PDFs and text files.
- Implemented centralized document processing based on MIME type.
- Updated `processMessage` to integrate document processing and summary extraction.
- Enhanced logging for document processing and error handling.

Fixes #[issue-number] (if applicable)42:T184b,# 🚀 Streaming Support for ElizaOS Core Runtime

## Overview
This PR introduces comprehensive streaming support to the ElizaOS core runtime, enabling real-time, token-by-token responses from language models. This is a significant enhancement that improves user experience through faster perceived response times and enables new use cases like real-time transcription and audio streaming.

## Key Changes

### 1. Core Runtime Enhancements (`packages/core`)

#### New Streaming Types and Interfaces
- **`ModelStream<T>`**: Type alias for `AsyncIterable<T>` representing streaming data
- **`ModelStreamHandler`**: Interface for registering streaming model implementations
- **Stream Chunk Types**: 
  - `TextStreamChunk`: For text generation streaming (delta events with partial text)
  - `TranscriptionStreamChunk`: For audio transcription streaming (partial transcripts)
  - `TextToSpeechStreamChunk`: For TTS streaming (audio chunks)
  - Base types: `ModelStreamFinishChunk`, `ModelStreamErrorChunk`, `ModelStreamUsageChunk`

#### Runtime Implementation
- **Stream Registry**: New `streamModels` Map to store streaming handlers by model type
- **`registerModelStream()`**: Register streaming handlers with priority-based resolution
- **`getModelStream()`**: Retrieve the highest-priority streaming handler for a model type
- **Stream Normalization**: `wrapReadableStream()` utility that normalizes different stream types:
  - Native `AsyncIterable` objects
  - Web `ReadableStream` API
  - Node.js `Readable` streams

#### Unified `useModel` API
Instead of adding a separate `useModelStream` function, streaming is elegantly integrated into the existing `useModel` API through overloads:

```typescript
// Non-streaming (default)
const result = await runtime.useModel(ModelType.TEXT_LARGE, { prompt: "Hello" });

// Streaming via event parameter
const stream = await runtime.useModel(
  ModelType.TEXT_LARGE, 
  { prompt: "Hello" },
  'STREAMING_TEXT'
);

for await (const chunk of stream) {
  if (chunk.event === 'delta') {
    console.log(chunk.delta); // Partial text
  }
}
```

### 2. OpenAI Plugin Integration (`plugin-openai`)

#### Streaming Implementations
- **Text Generation**: Uses `@ai-sdk/openai`'s `streamText` for GPT models
  - Yields delta chunks with partial text
  - Includes usage statistics (token counts)
  - Proper finish events with complete output
  
- **Text-to-Speech**: Streaming audio generation
  - Yields audio chunks for real-time playback
  - Fallback to single chunk if response isn't streamable

#### Type Safety
- All streaming handlers are fully typed with no `any` casts
- Local type definitions to handle module resolution
- Conditional registration based on runtime capabilities

### 3. Testing Infrastructure

#### Core Streaming Tests (`packages/core/src/__tests__/streaming.test.ts`)
- Tests for streaming handler registration and priority resolution
- Fallback behavior when no streaming handler exists
- Event emission during streaming
- Proper async iteration over stream chunks

### 4. Type Safety Improvements

#### Complete Type Coverage
- **No more `any` types**: All parameters and returns are properly typed
- **Generic constraints**: Using TypeScript generics to maintain type relationships
- **Mapped types**: `ModelParamsMap`, `ModelResultMap`, `ModelStreamChunkMap` for type-safe model operations
- **Overloaded signatures**: Clean API with proper return type inference

#### Fixed Issues
- Tokenizer parameters now include required `modelType` field
- Proper type assertions only where necessary (stream type detection)
- All explicit casts removed in favor of proper typing

## Benefits

### For Users
- **Faster Time-to-First-Token**: Users see responses begin immediately
- **Better UX**: Progressive loading instead of waiting for complete responses
- **Real-time Features**: Enables live transcription, streaming audio, etc.

### For Developers
- **Simple API**: Streaming integrated into existing `useModel` function
- **Type Safety**: Full TypeScript support with no `any` types
- **Flexibility**: Support for different stream formats and sources
- **Extensibility**: Easy to add new streaming model types

## Technical Highlights

### Stream Event Types
```typescript
// Text streaming example
{ event: 'delta', delta: 'Hello' }
{ event: 'delta', delta: ' world' }
{ event: 'usage', tokens: { prompt: 5, completion: 2, total: 7 } }
{ event: 'finish', output: 'Hello world' }
```

### Error Handling
- Graceful fallback to non-streaming when handlers unavailable
- Proper error propagation through stream chunks
- Abort signal support for cancellation

### Performance
- Minimal overhead for non-streaming calls
- Efficient stream normalization without buffering
- Priority-based handler selection for optimal provider choice

## Breaking Changes
None! The implementation is fully backward compatible:
- Existing `useModel` calls work unchanged
- Streaming is opt-in via the event parameter
- Plugins without streaming support continue to work

## Migration Guide
To enable streaming in your code:

```typescript
// Before (still works)
const response = await runtime.useModel(ModelType.TEXT_LARGE, {
  prompt: "Write a story"
});

// After (with streaming)
const stream = await runtime.useModel(
  ModelType.TEXT_LARGE,
  { prompt: "Write a story" },
  'STREAMING_TEXT'
);

for await (const chunk of stream) {
  if (chunk.event === 'delta') {
    process.stdout.write(chunk.delta);
  }
}
```

## Testing
- ✅ All existing tests pass
- ✅ New streaming-specific tests added
- ✅ Type checking passes with no errors
- ✅ No regression in non-streaming functionality

## Future Enhancements
- WebSocket/SSE transport for browser clients
- Streaming support for more model types (embeddings, image generation)
- Stream transformation utilities (buffering, throttling)
- Progress indicators for long-running streams

---

This implementation provides a robust, type-safe foundation for streaming in ElizaOS while maintaining full backward compatibility and excellent developer experience.
43:Te9d,## Add Sessions API to API Client SDK

### Summary
This PR adds support for the new Sessions API to the `@elizaos/api-client` package. The Sessions API provides a simplified interface for managing stateful conversations between users and agents, with automatic session management and cleanup.

### What's New
- **SessionsService**: New service class for all session-related operations
- **Type Definitions**: Complete TypeScript types for session management
- **Full API Coverage**: All session endpoints are implemented
- **Documentation**: Comprehensive API documentation and usage examples
- **Tests**: Unit tests covering all SessionsService methods

### Key Features
1. **Session Management**
   - Create and manage conversation sessions
   - Automatic session cleanup for inactive conversations
   - Session metadata support for platform-specific data

2. **Message Handling**
   - Send messages within sessions
   - Retrieve message history with pagination
   - Support for attachments and metadata

3. **Administrative Functions**
   - List all active sessions
   - Health check endpoint
   - Session deletion

### API Methods
```typescript
client.sessions.createSession(params)     // Create a new session
client.sessions.getSession(sessionId)     // Get session details
client.sessions.sendMessage(sessionId, params)  // Send a message
client.sessions.getMessages(sessionId, params)  // Get messages with pagination
client.sessions.deleteSession(sessionId)  // Delete a session
client.sessions.listSessions()            // List all active sessions
client.sessions.checkHealth()             // Check service health
```

### Usage Example
```typescript
import { ElizaClient } from '@elizaos/api-client';

const client = new ElizaClient({
  baseUrl: 'http://localhost:3000',
  apiKey: 'your-api-key',
});

// Create a session
const session = await client.sessions.createSession({
  agentId: 'agent-uuid',
  userId: 'user-uuid',
  metadata: { platform: 'web' }
});

// Send a message
const message = await client.sessions.sendMessage(session.sessionId, {
  content: 'Hello, agent!',
  attachments: [{
    type: 'image',
    url: 'https://example.com/image.jpg',
    name: 'screenshot.jpg'
  }]
});

// Get message history
const messages = await client.sessions.getMessages(session.sessionId, {
  limit: 50,
  before: new Date()
});
```

None. This is a purely additive change that doesn't affect existing functionality.

## Issues Fixed

### 1. **Incorrect v1 compatibility detection**
- **Problem**: Packages were marked as v1-compatible based solely on having a v1.x version published, regardless of their `@elizaos/core` dependency
- **Example**: `@elizaos/plugin-hedera` was incorrectly marked as v1-compatible despite not having proper core dependency

### 2. **Script crashes with dependency strings**
- **Problem**: Raw dependency strings like `"latest"`, `"0.25.6-alpha.1"` caused `semver.satisfies()` to crash
- **Solution**: Added `normalizeDependencyRange()` and `safelyCheckSemverSatisfies()` helpers

## Key Changes

✅ **Enhanced NPM package inspection**: Now extracts `@elizaos/core` dependency ranges from package.json  
✅ **Fixed NPM fallback logic**: Validates both version AND core dependency constraints  
✅ **Added semver normalization**: Handles `"latest"` → `">=0.0.0"`, exact versions → ranges  
✅ **Added error handling**: Prevents crashes with invalid dependency strings  
✅ **Regenerated registry**: With corrected v1 compatibility flags  

## Verification

- ✅ **No crashes**: Script completes successfully with all dependency string types
- ✅ **Correct v1 detection**: `@elizaos/plugin-hedera` now properly marked as `v1: false`
- ✅ **"latest" handling**: Packages with `"latest"` dependencies correctly evaluated
- ✅ **Exact version handling**: Dependencies like `"0.25.6-alpha.1"` converted to ranges and evaluated properly

## Impact

- **Eliminates false positives**: Packages without proper `@elizaos/core >= 1.0.0` dependency no longer incorrectly marked as v1-compatible
- **Prevents crashes**: Robust handling of all dependency string formats
- **Improves accuracy**: v1 compatibility now strictly based on actual core dependency satisfaction

This ensures the registry accurately reflects which plugins are truly compatible with v1 of the elizaOS core.45:T650,## Description

### Problem
The `elizaos publish` command incorrectly handled commas when adding new plugin entries to the registry's `index.json` file:
- Did not add a comma to the previously last entry
- Incorrectly added a comma to the new entry when it became the last entry

This resulted in invalid JSON:
```json
"@elizaos/plugin-action-bench": "github:elizaos-plugins/plugin-action-bench"
"plugin-fal-ai": "github:yungalgo/plugin-fal-ai",
```

### Solution
Modified the insertion logic in `publishToGitHub` function to:
1. Detect if inserting before the closing `}` brace (last entry position)
2. When inserting as last entry:
   - Add comma to the previous entry if it doesn't have one
   - Remove comma from the new entry
3. When inserting in the middle: keep comma on new entry

### Result
Produces valid JSON:
```json
"@elizaos/plugin-action-bench": "github:elizaos-plugins/plugin-action-bench",
"plugin-fal-ai": "github:yungalgo/plugin-fal-ai"
```

### Changes
- `packages/cli/src/utils/publisher.ts`: Updated comma handling logic in lines 444-470

- Convert logger calls across the repo to object-first structured logging to align with pino typings and fix TS/DTS errors.
- No functional behavior changes; improves type-safety and log structure.

### Why
- Recent stricter typings select the “message-only” overload when a string is first, causing TS2345 when a second argument is passed.
- Pino’s canonical usage is `logger.<level>(obj, msg)` for structured data.

### What changed
- Repo-wide refactor: replace string-first + extra arg with object-first structured calls; avoid passing arrays/objects as the second arg.
- Updated event/provider logs to attach data via an object, not as additional args.

### Affected packages
- core: minimal/no change in this pass
- server: converted error/warn/info usages in `packages/server/src/index.ts`
- cli: multiple files under `packages/cli/src/**` (start/test/update/utils), including top-level `src/index.ts`
- plugin-bootstrap: actions/providers/services/evaluators and `src/index.ts`
- plugin-dummy-services: `src/tokenData/service.ts`
- plugin-starter: `src/plugin.ts` (action error and event param keys)
- plugin-quick-starter: `src/plugin.ts` (event message/world logs)
- project-starter:
  - `src/index.ts` (character name log)
  - `src/plugin.ts` (action error, events param keys)
  - tests under `src/__tests__/*.ts` updated to structured error logs
- project-tee-starter: `src/plugin.ts` (keypair/signature logs, TEE warnings, event message preview)

### Pattern (before → after)
```ts
// Before (string-first + extra arg)
logger.error('Error in action:', error);
logger.debug('Message:', params.message);
logger.info('[EVENT]', previewText);

// After (object-first)
logger.error({ error }, 'Error in action:');
logger.debug({ message: params.message }, 'Message:');
logger.info({ preview: previewText }, '[EVENT]');
```

### Impact
- Fixes TS2345/“never” overload errors during DTS builds (observed in plugin-starter, plugin-quick-starter, project-starter, project-tee-starter, etc.).
- Structured logs (JSON) with explicit keys; log parsing/search may improve. Some plain appended values move into fields (e.g., `message`, `world`, `signature`, `keys`, `error`).

### Testing
- Type-checks pass locally on edited files; DTS errors reported in build output addressed at call sites noted above.
- No runtime logic changed. Only log call signatures and shapes.

### Guidance for contributors
- Use object-first logging whenever attaching data:
  - Prefer: `logger.warn({ context }, 'message')`
  - Avoid: `logger.warn('message', context)` or passing arrays/objects as the second arg.
- For previews, attach as a named field: `{ preview: text.slice(0, 50) }`.4c:Tde7,# Character Type System and Jesse Pollak Character Implementation

---

This PR establishes the foundation for a robust character system while providing immediate value through the specialized Jesse Pollak character for Base ecosystem support.4d:Tb4f,## 🐛 Fix: NPM Deployment Path Resolution Issue

### Problem
When the CLI is published to NPM and installed globally (e.g., `bun i -g @elizaos/cli`), it fails with the error:
```
Cannot find module '/home/runner/work/eliza/eliza/packages/cli/package.json'
```

This occurs because the build process in GitHub Actions CI/CD embeds absolute paths from the build machine, which don't exist on users' machines.

### Root Cause
The CLI was using **build-time path resolution** with `require.resolve('@elizaos/cli/package.json')` which gets hardcoded to the GitHub Actions runner path during the build process.

### Solution
Changed all path resolution to happen at **runtime** with multiple fallback strategies to handle different installation scenarios:
- ✅ Global NPM/Bun installations
- ✅ Local node_modules (as dependency)
- ✅ Monorepo development
- ✅ Various package manager locations

### Changes Made

#### 1. **`src/utils/copy-template.ts`**
- Added `getGlobalNodeModulesPaths()` helper to detect global installation directories
- Replaced `require.resolve()` with runtime path resolution
- Added fallback paths for templates and package.json

#### 2. **`src/utils/display-banner.ts`**
- Implemented multi-strategy version detection:
  1. Check monorepo context
  2. Check `ELIZAOS_CLI_VERSION` env var (embedded during build)
  3. Try multiple package.json locations
  4. Extract from installation path as fallback
- Returns `'unknown'` instead of crashing when package.json not found

#### 3. **`build.ts`**
- Reads version from package.json at build time
- Embeds version as `process.env.ELIZAOS_CLI_VERSION`
- Copies package.json to dist/ for runtime access

#### 4. **`package.json`**
- Added `dist/package.json` to files array for NPM package inclusion

#### 5. **`src/utils/user-environment.ts`**
- Updated CLI info detection with runtime fallbacks
- Silent failures (no warnings) as this is expected in some environments

### Testing
```bash
# Build and pack
cd packages/cli
bun run build
npm pack

# Test installation
cd /tmp
bun i -g ./elizaos-cli-*.tgz
elizaos create  # Should work without path errors
```

### Verification
- [x] No hardcoded `/home/runner` paths in built files
- [x] Works in monorepo development
- [x] Works when installed globally via NPM/Bun
- [x] Works as local dependency
- [x] Graceful fallbacks when paths not found

### Impact
- Fixes #[issue-number] - CLI fails when installed from NPM
- No breaking changes
- Backward compatible with existing installations

**Note to reviewers:** The key insight is that all path resolution must happen at runtime, not build time, to support the various ways users might install and run the CLI.4e:Tfd2,## Session Metadata Propagation for Plugin Actions

### Overview
This PR implements session metadata propagation throughout the ElizaOS message processing pipeline, enabling plugins and actions to access custom session metadata (like `ethAddress`, authentication tokens, or platform-specific data) that was provided during session creation.

### Problem
Previously, when creating a session with custom metadata via the REST API, this metadata was not accessible to plugin actions. This was particularly problematic for Web3 integrations where wallet addresses and other user context needed to persist across the session.

### Solution
Implemented a complete metadata propagation flow from session creation through to action handlers:

```
Session Creation → Channel Metadata → Message Metadata → Memory Object → Action Handler
```

### Changes Made

#### 1. **Session API Enhancement** (`packages/server/src/api/messaging/sessions.ts`)
- Modified message sending to fetch and merge channel metadata (containing session metadata)
- Ensures session metadata is included in all messages sent within that session

#### 2. **Message Bus Service Update** (`packages/server/src/services/message.ts`)
- Updated `createAgentMemory` to preserve all message metadata in Memory objects
- Session metadata now flows through to actions via `message.metadata`

#### 3. **Comprehensive Test Coverage**
- ✅ Added test: "should propagate session metadata to messages" in `sessions.test.ts`
- ✅ Added test: "should preserve session metadata when creating memories" in `message-bus.test.ts`
- ✅ Added test: "should handle messages without metadata gracefully" in `message-bus.test.ts`

#### 4. **Documentation & Examples**
- 📝 Created example action demonstrating metadata access (`examples/session-metadata-access.ts`)
- 📚 Added comprehensive documentation (`docs/session-metadata-propagation.md`)

### Usage Example

**Creating a session with metadata:**
```typescript
POST /api/messaging/sessions
{
  "agentId": "agent-123",
  "userId": "user-456",
  "metadata": {
    "ethAddress": "0x1234567890123456789012345678901234567890",
    "platform": "web3",
    "userPlan": "premium"
  }
}
```

**Accessing in your action:**
```typescript
handler: async (runtime: IAgentRuntime, message: Memory): Promise<boolean> => {
  const metadata = message.metadata || {};
  const ethAddress = metadata.ethAddress; // ✅ Now accessible!
  const sessionId = metadata.sessionId;
  
  if (ethAddress) {
    logger.info(`Processing Web3 request for wallet: ${ethAddress}`);
    // Your Web3 logic here
  }
  
  return true;
}
```

### Use Cases
- 🔐 **Web3 Integration**: Pass wallet addresses and chain preferences
- 🎫 **Authentication**: Include auth tokens and user permissions
- 🌍 **Platform Context**: Store platform-specific information (Discord server ID, etc.)
- ⚙️ **User Preferences**: Persist language, timezone, output format across messages

### Testing
All tests pass ✅
```bash
bun test --filter metadata  # Run metadata-specific tests
bun test sessions.test.ts  # Run session tests
bun test message-bus.test.ts  # Run message bus tests
```

### Breaking Changes
None - This implementation is fully backward compatible.

### Migration Guide
For existing implementations:
1. Update session creation to include metadata
2. Modify actions to read from `message.metadata`
3. No changes required if not using metadata

- [x] Documentation updated

- [x] No breaking changes

51:Tb2e,## Description

This PR fixes two minor issues preventing the tee command from working as intended:

### 1. Phala CLI Wrapper Argument Handling

The ElizaOS wrapper for the Phala CLI was not correctly capturing arguments, causing commands like `elizaos tee phala cvms create --image ...` to fail with "error: too many arguments for 'phala'. Expected 0 arguments but got X."

**Root Cause**: The wrapper's action handler was accessing `command.args` which was undefined. Commander.js requires specific configuration to capture variadic arguments. i think we probably wrote this before we had commander.

**Fix**: Added proper variadic argument handling:
```typescript
.allowExcessArguments(true)
.argument('[args...]', 'All arguments to pass to Phala CLI')
.action(async (...commandArgs) => {
    const args = Array.isArray(commandArgs[0]) ? commandArgs[0] : [];
    // ... rest of handler
})
```

### 2. TEE Starter Project Docker Build Failure

The project-tee-starter template was importing test files in production code, causing Docker builds to fail.

**Root Cause**: The `index.ts` was importing `ProjectTeeStarterTestSuite` from the `__tests__` directory, which is typically excluded in production builds.

**Fix**: Removed the test import and replaced the `tests` export with the proper `plugins` export:
```diff
- import ProjectTeeStarterTestSuite from './__tests__/e2e/project-tee-starter.e2e';
...
- tests: [ProjectTeeStarterTestSuite], // Export tests from ProjectAgent
+ plugins: [teeStarterPlugin], // Add any additional plugins here
```

## Testing

### Phala CLI Testing

# All commands now work correctly:
elizaos tee phala --help
elizaos tee phala status
elizaos tee phala auth login <key>
elizaos tee phala cvms create --image yungalgorithm/my-lil-agent:latest -e .env
```

### TEE Starter Build Testing
```bash

cd packages/project-tee-starter
docker build -t test-tee-starter .
```

## Impact

- Users can now use all Phala CLI commands through the ElizaOS wrapper

- No breaking changes to existing functionality

This PR adds a GitHub Actions workflow that enables Claude Code integration in our repository.

### What is Claude Code?

[Claude Code](https://claude.ai/code) is an AI coding agent that can help with:
- Bug fixes and improvements  
- Documentation updates
- Implementing new features
- Code reviews and suggestions
- Writing tests
- And more!

### How it works

Once this PR is merged, we'll be able to interact with Claude by mentioning @claude in a pull request or issue comment.
Once the workflow is triggered, Claude will analyze the comment and surrounding context, and execute on the request in a GitHub action.

### Important Notes

- **This workflow won't take effect until this PR is merged**
- **@claude mentions won't work until after the merge is complete**
- The workflow runs automatically whenever Claude is mentioned in PR or issue comments
- Claude gets access to the entire PR or issue context including files, diffs, and previous comments

### Security

- Our Anthropic API key is securely stored as a GitHub Actions secret
- Only users with write access to the repository can trigger the workflow
- All Claude runs are stored in the GitHub Actions run history
- Claude's default tools are limited to reading/writing files and interacting with our repo by creating comments, branches, and commits.
- We can add more allowed tools by adding them to the workflow file like:

```
allowed_tools: Bash(npm install),Bash(npm run build),Bash(npm run lint),Bash(npm run test)
```

There's more information in the [Claude Code action repo](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code-action).

After merging this PR, let's try mentioning @claude in a comment on any PR to get started!

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->58:T64b,## Description

This PR fixes the Windows CI test failures that were occurring due to how cmd.exe handles quoted file paths in commands.

## Problem

The tests were failing on Windows when commands contained file paths with spaces that were wrapped in quotes. The issue was specifically in the `bunExecSync` function in `bun-test-helpers.ts`.

## Solution

**Updated Approach**: Instead of trying to make shell quoting work correctly on Windows, we now bypass the shell entirely when the command contains quotes on Windows. This prevents the quotes from being passed through to the application as part of the argument value.

1. **Detect quoted commands on Windows** and bypass shell mode
2. **Parse the command manually** using our existing `parseCommand` function
3. **Pass arguments directly to Bun.spawn** without shell interpretation

## Changes Made

- Modified `bunExecSync` to detect when to bypass shell on Windows
- When bypassing shell, use `parseCommand` to properly handle quoted arguments
- Enhanced error messages to include exit code, working directory, and separate stdout/stderr
- Added path normalization utility that could be useful for future cross-platform compatibility
- Fixed type annotations to work with Bun's current API

## Testing

- All existing tests pass on macOS/Linux
- The specific failing tests now pass:
  - `agent get with output flag saves to file`
  - `agent set updates configuration correctly`
  - `agent start loads character from file`

## Related Issue

Fixes the Windows CI failures seen in https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/17056294220/job/4835452670159:Tebf,
# Relates to

This PR addresses an issue with the plugin installation logic, as tracked in the branch issue/plugin-install-fix.

# Risks

Low. The changes are confined to the plugin installation script within the CLI. The potential risk is that installing a plugin via the CLI might fail, but it should not affect any other part of the system.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

This PR updates the logic for installing plugins using the CLI to ensure greater reliability.

## What kind of change is this?

Improvement (misc. changes to existing features)


## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?
Previously, when installing a plugin from a GitHub URL, the CLI would assume the plugin's package name was the same as the repository name (e.g., elizaOS/plugin-xyz would be assumed to be plugin-xyz). This assumption is often incorrect, as the actual name field in the plugin's package.json can be different (e.g., @eliza/plugin-xyz).
This led to problems in post-installation steps, like setting up environment variables, because the CLI was looking for the wrong package name in the project's dependencies.
This PR fixes that. Now, after installing the plugin, the code reads the project's package.json file to find the actual package name that was just added. This makes the process more robust and removes the faulty assumption, ensuring that post-installation steps work correctly regardless of how the plugin is named.

# Documentation changes needed?

My changes do not require a change to the project documentation.


# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

A reviewer should start by examining the diff in packages/cli/src/commands/plugins/actions/install.ts.

## Detailed testing steps

From the eliza package root, attempt to install a plugin using the CLI.
Run the command: bun eliza plugins:install <plugin-name> (e.g., bun eliza plugins:install @eliza-plugins/dummy-services).
Verify that the command completes successfully and the plugin is added to the project's dependencies.
Run a command from the newly installed plugin to ensure it was loaded correctly.

OLD****

5a:T584,# Background

## What does this PR do?

Introduce entity for cancelling runs `CancelRunSignal`

## Why are we doing this? Any context or related work?

- Calls to LLMs usually cost some credits
- When we receive the `RUN_STARTED` event we can check current entity and end run to prevent credits spending

# Documentation changes needed?

## Methods

- `static getSignal(runId: UUID)` - get signal by runId or create if null
- `static clear(runId: UUID)` - clear signal from map
- `cancel(content?: Content)` - cancel current run and and send `content` as a response to user

## Example

```typescript
import { Content, EventType, Plugin } from "@elizaos/core";
import { CancelRunSignal } from "@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap";

// consider we have a service to determine eligibility for entity
interface EligibilityService {
  check(entityId: UUID): Promise<{ result: boolean, reason?: Content }>
}

const plugin: Plugin = {
...
  events: {
    [EventType.RUN_STARTED]: ({ entityId, runId, runtime }) => {
      const service = runtime.getService<EligibilityService>("USER_MANAGEMENT");
      const { result, reason } = await service.check(entityId);
      
      if (!result) {
        const signal = CancelRunSignal.getSignal(runId);
        signal.cancel(reason);
      }
    },
    ...
  },
  ...
}

export default Plugin;
```

This PR adds support for viewing intermediate tool calls and results in the chat completions API while maintaining full OpenAI API compliance.

## Changes

- **OpenAI API Compliance**: Default responses remain fully compliant with OpenAI's chat completions API
- **Optional Tool Call Visibility**: Add `include_intermediate_steps` parameter to expose tool execution flow
- **Proper Tool Call Formatting**: Tool calls follow OpenAI's exact format with `tool_calls` array
- **Tool Result Messages**: Tool results are returned as proper `tool` role messages with `tool_call_id`
- **Multi-step Execution**: Support for agents with multiple tool execution steps

## Usage

### Standard OpenAI Response (default)
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/v1/chat/completions \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"agent_id":"default","messages":[{"role":"user","content":"What is my wallet address?"}]}'
```

Returns standard OpenAI format:
```json
{
  "choices": [{
    "message": {
      "role": "assistant",
      "content": "Your wallet address is: 0x123...",
      "tool_calls": [...] // Only if tools were used
    },
    "finish_reason": "stop" // or "tool_calls"
  }]
}
```

### With Tool Call Visibility
```bash
curl -X POST http://localhost:3000/v1/chat/completions \
  -H 'Content-Type: application/json' \
  -d '{"agent_id":"default","messages":[{"role":"user","content":"What is my wallet address?"}],"include_intermediate_steps":true}'
```

Returns additional `conversation_flow` with complete execution trace:
```json
{
  "choices": [...],
  "conversation_flow": [
    {"role": "user", "content": "What is my wallet address?"},
    {"role": "assistant", "tool_calls": [{"id": "call_123", "function": {"name": "getWalletAddress"}}]},
    {"role": "tool", "tool_call_id": "call_123", "content": "{\"address\":\"0x123...\"}"},
    {"role": "assistant", "content": "Your wallet address is: 0x123..."}
  ]
}
```

## Benefits

- **Full OpenAI Compatibility**: Works with any OpenAI-compatible client
- **Complete Traceability**: See exactly what tools were called and their results
- **Debugging Support**: Understand agent decision-making process
- **Backward Compatible**: No breaking changes to existing API usage5e:T7e9,## Summary

This PR fixes a critical database error that was occurring during entity creation:

```
[ERROR] Error creating entity: Failed query: insert into "entities" values ($1, $2, default, default, default)
params: [only 2 parameters provided instead of expected 5]
```

## Root Cause

The issue was a mismatch between the TypeScript `Entity` interface and the database schema:
- **TypeScript**: `metadata?: Metadata` (optional)
- **Database**: `metadata JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'` (required with default)

When entities were created without metadata, Drizzle generated incorrect SQL with mismatched parameter counts.

## Solution

### 1. Type System Fix (Primary)
- Made `Entity.metadata` required in TypeScript to match database schema
- This prevents the issue at compile time rather than runtime

### 2. Logger Improvements (Secondary)  
- Converted structured logging calls to string-based messages
- Fixed TypeScript errors in logger parameter types
- Improved performance by eliminating object creation in log calls

## Benefits

- **Type Safety**: Compile-time prevention of entity creation without metadata
- **Performance**: No runtime data transformation needed
- **Consistency**: TypeScript types now match database reality
- **Maintainability**: Self-documenting code that makes requirements clear

## Testing

All entity-related tests pass:
- ✅ Basic entity creation and retrieval
- ✅ Entity updates and deletions  
- ✅ Multiple entity operations
- ✅ Edge cases (duplicate creation, missing data)
- ✅ Complex entity operations with metadata

## Migration Impact

This is a breaking change for any code creating entities without metadata, but:
1. The database already required metadata (with empty object default)
2. Most existing code already provides metadata
3. TypeScript will catch any missing cases at compile time

## Files Changed

- `packages/core/src/types/environment.ts`: Made Entity.metadata required
- `packages/plugin-sql/src/base.ts`: Removed runtime transformation, fixed logging5f:Ta00,## Summary

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->60:Tc8b,## 🔧 Fix: Type Export Issues in @elizaos/core Package

### Problem
The deployed version of `@elizaos/core` on NPM was experiencing type export failures, causing TypeScript compilation errors when the package was used outside the monorepo. Users reported errors like:
```
Module '"@elizaos/core"' has no exported member 'Agent'
Module '"@elizaos/core"' has no exported member 'UUID'
... (174+ similar errors)
```

### Root Cause Analysis
1. **Missing Source Files**: The published NPM package only included the `dist` folder, but the type declaration files referenced source files in `../src` that weren't included in the package
2. **Broken Type Generation**: The build process was attempting to generate TypeScript declarations using `npx tsc` which wasn't available in the build environment
3. **Incorrect Build Script**: The `build.ts` script created stub `.d.ts` files that pointed to non-existent locations

### Solution Implemented

#### 1. Fixed `package.json` exports and files
```diff
  "files": [
-   "dist"
+   "dist",
+   "src"
  ],
```

#### 2. Updated `build.ts` to generate proper type declaration stubs
```typescript
// dist/index.d.ts
export * from '../src/index.node';

// dist/node/index.d.ts  
export * from '../../src/index.node';

// dist/browser/index.d.ts
export * from '../../src/index.browser';
```

#### 3. Created `tsconfig.build.json` for future TypeScript compilation
```json
{
  "extends": "./tsconfig.json",
  "compilerOptions": {
    "declaration": true,
    "declarationMap": true,
    "emitDeclarationOnly": true,
    "outDir": "./dist",
    "rootDir": "./src"
  }
}
```

### Verification
✅ **Monorepo Development**: Types resolve correctly through source references
✅ **NPM Package**: Published package includes source files for type resolution
✅ **Build Output Structure**:
```
dist/
├── index.d.ts         # → ../src/index.node
├── index.js           # Conditional exports
├── node/
│   ├── index.d.ts     # → ../../src/index.node
│   └── index.node.js  # Compiled bundle
└── browser/
    ├── index.d.ts     # → ../../src/index.browser
    └── index.browser.js # Compiled bundle
```

### Testing
```typescript
// All types now import successfully:
import { 
  Agent, UUID, Character, Memory, IAgentRuntime,
  Plugin, Action, Provider, Service, logger
} from '@elizaos/core';
```

### Impact
- **Before**: Package worked only in monorepo, failed when published to NPM
- **After**: Package works in both monorepo development and NPM distribution
- **No Breaking Changes**: Maintains backward compatibility with existing code

### Build Commands
```bash
# Clean and rebuild
bun run clean
bun run build

# The build now properly generates:
# - JavaScript bundles for node and browser
# - Type declaration stubs that reference source files
# - Source maps for debugging
```

### Notes for Reviewers
- The solution includes source files in the NPM package, which is a common pattern for TypeScript packages
- Alternative approach would be to properly generate `.d.ts` files during build, but that requires additional tooling setup
- This fix is minimal, non-breaking, and immediately resolves the critical issue

<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->67:T6d8,## Description

This PR fixes failing component tests in the project-starter template by replacing `mock.module` with `spyOn` for logger mocking.

## Problem

The project-starter template had 3 test files using `mock.module('@elizaos/core')` which completely replaced the logger module, breaking Pino's structured logging behavior. This caused tests to fail when expecting structured log calls like:

```typescript
logger.info({ keys: Object.keys(params) }, 'MESSAGE_RECEIVED param keys');
```

## Solution

Replace `mock.module` with `spyOn` in the following files:
- `src/__tests__/events.test.ts`
- `src/__tests__/error-handling.test.ts`
- `src/__tests__/config.test.ts`

This aligns with the testing approach used in all other starter templates and preserves the original logger behavior while still allowing test assertions.

## Testing

- ✅ All component tests now pass (`elizaos test --type component`)
- ✅ Verified consistency across all starter templates
- ✅ No behavior changes to the actual plugin code

## Impact

This is a test-only fix with no impact on runtime behavior. It ensures developers creating new projects with `elizaos create` will have all tests passing out of the box (as a follow-up to PR #5705 )

## Summary
This PR updates the `AgentLog` interface structure in the API client types and fixes the corresponding mapping function in the client package to ensure the action viewer displays correct information.

## Changes Made

### API Client Types (`packages/api-client/src/types/agents.ts`)
- **Updated `AgentLog` interface**: Changed from a simple log structure to a more comprehensive one that includes:
  - Optional fields for better flexibility (`id?`, `type?`, `timestamp?`, etc.)
  - New `body` object containing model information (`modelType`, `modelKey`, `params`, `response`, `usage`)
  - Support for additional metadata fields with `[key: string]: any`

### Client Package (`packages/client/src/lib/api-type-mappers.ts`)
- **Fixed `mapApiLogToClient` function**: Updated the mapping logic to work with the new `AgentLog` structure:
  - Changed `type` mapping from `apiLog.metadata?.type || apiLog.level` to `apiLog?.type || apiLog.body?.modelType`
  - Updated timestamp handling to use the new structure
  - Fixed field mappings to match the updated interface

### CLI Package (`packages/cli/package.json`)
- **Dependency updates**: Added `@elizaos/plugin-anthropic` and reordered dependencies for consistency

## Technical Details

The main issue was that the `AgentLog` interface had evolved to support more detailed model usage information (like token counts, model types, etc.), but the client-side mapping function was still using the old structure. This caused the action viewer to fail to display correct information about agent actions and model usage.

The new structure better supports:
- Model type identification (`modelType`, `modelKey`)
- Usage tracking (`prompt_tokens`, `completion_tokens`, `total_tokens`)
- Flexible metadata storage
- Better timestamp handling

## Testing
- [x] API client types compile correctly
- [x] Client mapping function works with new log structure
- [x] Action viewer displays correct information
- [x] No breaking changes to existing functionality

## Breaking Changes
None - this is a forward-compatible update that enhances the existing functionality.

## Related Issues
Fixes action viewer display issues caused by outdated `AgentLog` type mapping.69:T7f4,This PR fixes two minor issues with the `elizaos publish` command:

**1. Fix `elizaos publish --test` failing out of the box**

When running `elizaos publish --test` OOTB, we get an error:
```
[2025-08-13 06:54:20] ERROR: Failed to update file: 404 Not Found
[2025-08-13 06:54:20] ERROR: Repository or path not found: yungalgo/registry/test-files/.gitkeep/
```

**Root cause**: The `ensureDirectory` function was incorrectly passing parameters to `updateFile`. It was passing the full repository string (e.g., `"username/repo"`) as a single parameter, but `updateFile` expects separate `owner` and `repo` parameters.

**Fix**: Split the repository string into owner and repo components before calling `updateFile`.

**2. Remove GitHub repository references when using `--npm` flag**

When running `elizaos publish --npm`, we publish to npm only (skipping GitHub repo creation), but the package.json still included repository and bugs URLs pointing to a non-existent GitHub repository. This shows up on the npm package page as broken links.

**Root cause**: The publish flow was setting repository placeholders regardless of the --npm flag.

**Fix**: 
- Delete `repository` and `bugs` fields from package.json when `--npm` flag is used
- Make the `repository` field optional in `PackageMetadata` interface
- Only include repository in metadata when it actually exists

## Testing

- ✅ Tested `elizaos publish --test` - now completes successfully
- ✅ Tested `elizaos publish --npm` - package.json no longer includes phantom GitHub URLs
- ✅ Verified no regressions in standard `elizaos publish` flow

## Changes

- `packages/cli/src/utils/github.ts`: Fixed parameter passing in `ensureDirectory`
- `packages/cli/src/commands/publish/index.ts`: Added logic to remove repository/bugs fields for npm-only publishing
- `packages/cli/src/commands/publish/utils/metadata.ts`: Made repository field conditional
- `packages/cli/src/commands/publish/types.ts`: Made repository field optional in interface
6a:T7e3,## Problem

The `elizaos create` command was failing with a module resolution error when the CLI was installed globally:

```
Cannot find module '/home/runner/work/eliza/eliza/packages/cli/package.json' from '/Users/benjaminberta/.bun/install/global/node_modules/@elizaos/cli/dist/index.js'
```

This occurred because the `copyTemplate` function was using `require.resolve('@elizaos/cli/package.json')` to locate template files, but this was failing when the CLI was installed globally. The resolver was trying to find a hardcoded path from the build environment instead of the actual installed location.

## Solution

This PR fixes the issue by:

1. **Adding proper error handling** around `require.resolve()` calls
2. **Implementing fallback strategies** that try multiple template locations:
   - Direct paths relative to the current module  
   - Resolved paths from the CLI package (when available)
   - Fallback paths for different installation scenarios

3. **Making template resolution robust** by:
   - Wrapping `require.resolve()` in try-catch blocks
   - Providing multiple fallback locations
   - Logging debug information when resolution fails
   - Continuing execution even if some paths fail to resolve

## Changes

- Modified `packages/cli/src/utils/copy-template.ts` to handle module resolution failures gracefully
- Added fallback mechanisms for template path resolution
- Maintained backward compatibility with all installation methods
- Added debug logging for troubleshooting

## Testing

✅ Tested with globally installed CLI
✅ Verified project creation works correctly
✅ Confirmed all template files are properly copied and configured
✅ Maintained compatibility with existing installation methods

## Impact

- Fixes the "Cannot find module" error when using `elizaos create`
- Improves reliability of the CLI across different installation scenarios
- No breaking changes - fully backward compatible

This PR fixes the failing CLI test `update --check works` that was failing in CI due to version handling in monorepo context.

## Problem

The test was expecting a semantic version pattern (e.g., `1.2.0`) but was receiving `workspace:*` because the CLI's package.json in the monorepo uses workspace protocol.

## Solution

- Updated `getVersion()` in `version-utils.ts` to detect monorepo context using the standard `detectDirectoryType` pattern
- Returns `'monorepo'` instead of `'workspace:*'` when running in monorepo context
- Updated the test regex to accept both `'monorepo'` and semantic versions
- Follows existing patterns used throughout the codebase (e.g., in `dev-server.ts`, `build-utils.ts`)

## Changes

1. **packages/cli/src/commands/update/utils/version-utils.ts**
   - Added import for `detectDirectoryType`
   - Check for monorepo using `directoryInfo.type === 'elizaos-monorepo'`
   - Return `'monorepo'` when in monorepo context or when version is `workspace:*`

2. **packages/cli/tests/commands/update.test.ts**
   - Updated test regex to accept both `monorepo` and semantic versions
   - Added comment explaining the expected behavior

## Testing

- Manually tested `elizaos update --check` command - correctly returns `Version: monorepo`
- Test should now pass in CI environment

6e:T5e2,## Problem

When running any ElizaOS CLI command (like `elizaos create`), users see confusing environment warnings:

```
[ENV] No .env file found in any of the expected locations
[ENV] ⚠️ OPENAI_API_KEY not found in process.env
[ENV] Safe env preview: {"NODE_ENV":"<missing>","OPENAI_API_KEY":"<missing>"}
```

These warnings appear even for commands that don't need environment variables, like `elizaos create` which is setting up a new project that doesn't have a `.env` file yet.

## Root Cause

The issue was in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/runtime-factory.ts`:
- The module had a top-level call to `loadEnvironmentVariables()` that executed immediately when the module was imported
- The CLI's main entry point imports all commands at startup, including the scenario command
- The scenario command imports `runtime-factory.ts`
- This caused environment checking to happen for every CLI command, not just when needed

## Solution

Made the environment loading lazy by:
1. Removing the top-level `loadEnvironmentVariables()` call
2. Creating an `ensureEnvLoaded()` function that loads environment variables only when first accessed
3. Updating the code to use `ensureEnvLoaded()` instead of directly accessing `envSettings`

## Testing

Tested both:
- `elizaos create` - No environment warnings appear ✅
- `bun run packages/cli/dist/index.js create` - No environment warnings appear ✅

The environment variables are still loaded correctly when actually needed by the scenario command.6f:Td69,# Overall Project Monthly Summary (August 2025)

## Executive Summary
August was a month of strategic preparation and ecosystem growth. We focused on strengthening our core platform by improving the developer experience and cleaning up the codebase, while also seeing community-driven expansion with the submission of a new plugin to our registry. This foundational work prepares us for accelerated feature development and enhances the modularity central to our mission.

### Key Strategic Initiatives & Outcomes

**Strengthening the Foundation for Future Growth**
*Strategic Importance: A clean, efficient, and well-documented development environment allows our team and community to build new features faster, more reliably, and with greater confidence.*
- The developer setup process was streamlined, making it easier for new and existing contributors to get started on the project in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- We performed significant housekeeping by removing obsolete code, documentation, and automated workflows, which simplifies our codebase and makes our continuous integration (CI/CD) pipelines more efficient in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).
- Planning for future capabilities has begun, with proposals for a more modular storage system and a new sessions API that will enhance agent autonomy and adaptability in [elizaos/eliza](https://github.com/elizaos/eliza).

**Expanding the Ecosystem with New Capabilities**
*Strategic Importance: Growing our library of plugins is essential for making ElizaOS agents more capable and versatile, enabling them to tackle a wider range of complex tasks.*
- The community contributed a proposal to add a new knowledge graph plugin, `plugin-fluxpoint-kg`, which aims to expand the reasoning capabilities available to agents within the ElizaOS framework in [elizaos-plugins/registry](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry).

79:T1ce5,**Describe the bug**

`packages/cli/src/commands/start/actions/agent-start.ts` is exported and re-used in CLI commands with  

```ts
import { startAgent } from '../commands/start';
```

When I call `startAgent` from `runtime-factory.ts` / `initializeAgent()`:

```ts
const runtime = await startAgent(
  encryptedCharacter(character),
  server,
  undefined,
  [],                       // <-- intentionally no bootstrap plugin
  { isTestMode: false }
);
```

initialization hangs almost immediately (before plugin dependency resolution).

If I add `@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap` back:

```ts
const runtime = await startAgent(
  encryptedCharacter(character),
  server,
  undefined,
  ['@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap'],
  { isTestMode: false }
);
```

initialization gets past early steps, loads **all** plugins, but then hangs right after the bootstrap plugin finishes loading.

---

**To Reproduce**

1. Build the CLI (`cd packages/cli && bun x tsup`).
2. From `packages/cli` run a scenario that relies on `initializeAgent`, e.g.:

```bash
bun run src/index.ts scenario run \
  src/commands/scenario/examples/e2b-test.scenario.yaml
```

3. Edit `runtime-factory.ts` ➜ `initializeAgent()` and comment the bootstrap plugin in the `character.plugins` array (lines 411-415).
4. Re-run the same command – observe early hang.
5. Re-enable the bootstrap plugin and re-run – observe later hang.

---

**Expected behavior**

`startAgent` should finish initializing an agent regardless of whether `@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap` is present.  
If the bootstrap plugin is mandatory there should be a clear validation error, not a silent hang.

---

**Logs / Screenshots**

<details>
<summary>1️⃣ Hang without bootstrap plugin (early-stage)</summary>

```
[2025-08-04 02:47:47] INFO: [startAgent] Step 1 – Starting agent initialization
[2025-08-04 02:47:47] INFO: [startAgent] Step 2 – Character ID set

const importStrategies: ImportStrategy[] = [
  // Try local development first - this is the most important for plugin testing
  {
    name: 'local development plugin',
    tryImport: async (repository: string) => {
      const context = detectPluginContext(repository);

      if (context.isLocalDevelopment) {
        logger.debug(`Detected local development for plugin: ${repository}`);

        // Ensure the plugin is built
        const isBuilt = await ensurePluginBuilt(context);
        if (!isBuilt) {
          provideLocalPluginGuidance(repository, context);
          return null;
        }

        // Try to load from built output
        if (context.localPath && existsSync(context.localPath)) {
          logger.info(`Loading local development plugin: ${repository}`);
          return tryImporting(context.localPath, 'local development plugin', repository);
        }

        // This shouldn't happen if ensurePluginBuilt succeeded, but handle it gracefully
        logger.warn(`Plugin built but output not found at expected path: ${context.localPath}`);
        provideLocalPluginGuidance(repository, context);
        return null;
      }

      return null;

    tryImport: async (repository: string) => {
      if (repository.startsWith('@elizaos/plugin-')) {
        // Try to find the plugin in the workspace
        const pluginName = repository.replace('@elizaos/', '');
        const workspacePath = path.resolve(process.cwd(), '..', pluginName, 'dist', 'index.js');
        if (existsSync(workspacePath)) {
          return tryImporting(workspacePath, 'workspace dependency', repository);
        }

    tryImport: async (repository: string) => tryImporting(repository, 'direct path', repository),

    name: 'local node_modules',
    tryImport: async (repository: string) =>
      tryImporting(resolveNodeModulesPath(repository), 'local node_modules', repository),

      const globalPath = path.resolve(getGlobalNodeModulesPath(), repository);
      if (!existsSync(path.dirname(globalPath))) {
        logger.debug(
          `Global node_modules directory not found at ${path.dirname(globalPath)}, skipping for ${repository}`
        );
        return null;
      }
      return tryImporting(globalPath, 'global node_modules', repository);
    },

      const packageJson = await readPackageJson(repository);
      if (!packageJson) return null;

      const entryPoint = packageJson.module || packageJson.main || DEFAULT_ENTRY_POINT;
      return tryImporting(
        resolveNodeModulesPath(repository, entryPoint),
        `package.json entry (${entryPoint})`,
        repository

      const packageJson = await readPackageJson(repository);
      if (packageJson?.main === DEFAULT_ENTRY_POINT) return null;

      return tryImporting(
        resolveNodeModulesPath(repository, DEFAULT_ENTRY_POINT),
        'common dist pattern',
        repository
      );

## Description

This ticket enhances the mocking system to support conditional responses based on input parameters and complex response structures with metadata. This enables realistic testing of service interactions like GitHub API calls or EVM transactions with proper request/response matching.

## Acceptance Criteria

1. Mock definitions support `when` clauses for conditional responses
2. `when` clauses can match on method arguments, input parameters, or request context
3. Mock responses support complex nested structures with metadata (timestamps, IDs, etc.)
4. Multiple mock responses can be defined for the same service/method with different conditions
5. Mock system provides clear logging of which mock was triggered and why
6. Mock responses can include realistic error conditions and edge cases
7. Support for dynamic response generation based on input parameters
8. Mock validation ensures `when` clauses are syntactically correct

## Technical Approach

### 1. Enhanced Mock Schema
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
const MockSchema = z.object({
  service: z.string(),
  method: z.string(),
  when: z.object({
    // Match on method arguments
    args: z.array(z.any()).optional(),
    // Match on specific argument values
    input: z.record(z.any()).optional(),
    // Match on request context
    context: z.record(z.any()).optional(),
    // Custom matching function
    matcher: z.string().optional(), // JavaScript expression
  }).optional(),
  response: z.any(), // Can be function or static value
  // For dynamic responses
  responseFn: z.string().optional(), // JavaScript function
  // Error simulation
  error: z.object({
    code: z.string(),
    message: z.string(),
  }).optional(),
});
```

### 2. Mock Engine Implementation
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/mock-engine.ts
export class MockEngine {
  private mocks: MockDefinition[] = [];

  addMock(mock: MockDefinition) {
    this.mocks.push(mock);
  }

  async findMock(service: string, method: string, args: any[]): Promise<any> {
    const candidates = this.mocks.filter(m => 
      m.service === service && m.method === method
    );

    for (const mock of candidates) {
      if (await this.matchesCondition(mock, args)) {
        this.logger.info(`Mock triggered: ${service}.${method} with condition: ${JSON.stringify(mock.when)}`);
        return this.generateResponse(mock, args);
      }
    }

    return null; // No mock found
  }

  private async matchesCondition(mock: MockDefinition, args: any[]): Promise<boolean> {
    if (!mock.when) return true; // Default mock

    // Match on arguments
    if (mock.when.args) {
      if (!this.deepEqual(args, mock.when.args)) return false;
    }

    // Match on input parameters
    if (mock.when.input) {
      const input = this.extractInputFromArgs(args);
      if (!this.deepEqual(input, mock.when.input)) return false;
    }

    // Custom matcher function
    if (mock.when.matcher) {
      const matcherFn = new Function('args', 'input', mock.when.matcher);
      return matcherFn(args, this.extractInputFromArgs(args));
    }

    return true;
  }

  private generateResponse(mock: MockDefinition, args: any[]): any {
    if (mock.error) {
      throw new Error(`${mock.error.code}: ${mock.error.message}`);
    }

    if (mock.responseFn) {
      const responseFn = new Function('args', 'input', mock.responseFn);
      return responseFn(args, this.extractInputFromArgs(args));
    }

    return mock.response;
  }
}
```

## Description

This ticket enables scenarios to test agent behavior through natural language interactions rather than direct code execution. This allows testing of agent reasoning, decision-making, and response generation in realistic conversation contexts with proper evaluation of agent responses.

## Acceptance Criteria

1. Scenario `run` blocks support `input` field for natural language prompts to agents
2. Agent responses are captured and available for evaluation (text, thoughts, actions)
3. Evaluators can access both agent response text and execution context
4. Support for multi-turn conversations in scenarios

## Technical Approach

### 1. Enhanced Run Step Schema
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
const RunStepSchema = z.object({
  name: z.string().optional(),
  // Natural language input to agent
  input: z.string().optional(),
  // Direct code execution (existing)
  lang: z.string().optional(),
  code: z.string().optional(),

    conversation_id: z.string().optional(),
    user_id: z.string().optional(),
    room_id: z.string().optional(),
  }).optional(),

});
```

### 2. Agent Interaction Engine
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/agent-interaction.ts
export class AgentInteractionEngine {
  constructor(private runtime: IAgentRuntime) {}

  async interactWithAgent(input: string, context?: AgentContext): Promise<AgentResponse> {
    // Create message for agent
    const message: Memory = {
      entityId: context?.user_id || 'scenario-user',
      roomId: context?.room_id || 'scenario-room',
      content: {
        type: 'text',
        text: input,
      },
      metadata: {
        type: 'message',
        conversationId: context?.conversation_id,
      },
    };

    // Send to agent and capture response
    const startTime = Date.now();
    const response = await this.runtime.processMessage(message);
    const endTime = Date.now();

    return {
      text: response.text,
      thoughts: response.thoughts,
      actions: response.actions,
      timing: {
        startTime,
        endTime,
        duration: endTime - startTime,
      },
      context: {
        conversationId: context?.conversation_id,
        messageId: message.id,
      },
    };
  }
}
```

### 3. Enhanced Execution Result
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/providers.ts
export interface ExecutionResult {
  exitCode: number;
  stdout: string;
  stderr: string;
  files: Record<string, string>;
  // New: Agent interaction results
  agentResponse?: AgentResponse;
  conversationHistory?: AgentResponse[];
}
```

## Description

This ticket implements plugin specification in scenario YAML files, allowing scenarios to declare which plugins are required for testing. This enables testing of agent behaviors that depend on specific plugins like `@elizaos/plugin-github` or `@elizaos/plugin-evm`. The system will dynamically load specified plugins during scenario execution and make their actions, providers, and services available to the agent.

## Acceptance Criteria

1. Scenario YAML supports a `plugins` array at the root level with string plugin names
2. The `initializeAgent()` function respects scenario plugin specifications and loads them via `startAgent()`
3. Plugin loading follows the same dependency resolution and error handling as the main CLI
4. Scenarios can specify both string plugin names (`@elizaos/plugin-github`) and direct plugin objects
5. Plugin loading errors are clearly reported with actionable guidance
6. Default plugins (bootstrap, sql) are automatically included unless explicitly excluded via `exclude_defaults: true`
7. Plugin conflicts are detected and reported during scenario validation
8. Plugin initialization errors don't crash the scenario but are reported in results

## Technical Approach

### 1. Update Scenario Schema
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/schema.ts
const ScenarioSchema = z.object({
  name: z.string(),
  description: z.string(),
  plugins: z.array(z.string()).optional(), // e.g., ["@elizaos/plugin-github"]
  exclude_defaults: z.boolean().optional(), // exclude bootstrap/sql
  environment: EnvironmentSchema,
  setup: SetupSchema.optional(),
  run: z.array(RunStepSchema),
  judgment: JudgmentSchema,
});
```

### 2. Enhance Runtime Factory
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/runtime-factory.ts
export async function initializeAgent(scenario: Scenario): Promise<IAgentRuntime> {
  const character: Character = {
    name: 'scenario-runner',
    id: stringToUuid('scenario-runner'),
    bio: 'A minimal character for running scenarios',
    plugins: scenario.plugins || []
  };

  // Load default plugins unless excluded
  if (!scenario.exclude_defaults) {
    character.plugins.push('@elizaos/plugin-bootstrap', '@elizaos/plugin-sql');
  }

  const runtime = await startAgent(
    encryptedCharacter(character),
    server,
    undefined,
    character.plugins,
    { isTestMode: true }
  );

  return runtime;
}
```

### 3. Plugin Validation
```typescript
// packages/cli/src/scenarios/plugin-validator.ts
export async function validateScenarioPlugins(scenario: Scenario): Promise<ValidationResult[]> {
  const results: ValidationResult[] = [];
  
  for (const pluginName of scenario.plugins || []) {
    try {
      const plugin = await loadAndPreparePlugin(pluginName);
      if (!plugin) {
        results.push({
          type: 'error',
          message: `Plugin '${pluginName}' could not be loaded`,
          suggestion: 'Check if plugin is installed or built correctly'
        });
      }
    } catch (error) {
      results.push({
        type: 'error', 
        message: `Failed to validate plugin '${pluginName}': ${error.message}`,
        suggestion: 'Verify plugin dependencies and configuration'
      });
    }
  }
  
  return results;
}
```

The `elizaos publish --npm` command reports successful publishing but the plugin is not actually published to npm, despite showing success messages. The npm publish is failing with a 404 error, but the ElizaOS CLI incorrectly reports success.

## Key Problems Identified

### 1. Critical NPM Publishing Failure
- **Error**: `npm error 404 Not Found - PUT https://registry.npmjs.org/@elizaos%2fplugin-polygon - Not found`
- **Issue**: The package scope `@elizaos` doesn't exist on npm, preventing publishing
- **Impact**: Package cannot be published to npm, but CLI reports success

### 2. False Success Reporting
- **Issue**: CLI reports `[√] Successfully published @elizaos/plugin-polygon@1.0.0 to npm`
- **Reality**: npm publish failed with 404 error
- **Impact**: Developers believe their plugin is published when it's not

### 3. Registry Repository Mismatch
- **Expected**: Should create PR against `https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/registry`
- **Actual**: Still references old registry `https://github.com/elizaos/registry`
- **Impact**: Plugin won't appear in the official ElizaOS registry

```bash

# Run the publish command

### NPM Error Analysis
The npm logs show the root cause:
```
http fetch PUT 404 https://registry.npmjs.org/@elizaos%2fplugin-polygon 507ms
error 404 Not Found - PUT https://registry.npmjs.org/@elizaos%2fplugin-polygon - Not found
```

This indicates that the `@elizaos` scope doesn't exist on npm, which is required for publishing scoped packages.

### CLI Error Handling Failure
The ElizaOS CLI is not properly parsing npm error codes:
- npm returns exit code 1 (failure)
- CLI ignores the error and reports success
- No validation that the package actually exists on npm

## Expected Behavior
1. CLI should detect npm publish failures (exit code 1)
2. CLI should fail with clear error message when package scope doesn't exist
3. CLI should validate that package actually exists on npm after publishing
4. CLI should create PRs against the correct registry repository

## Recommended Fixes

### 1. Fix NPM Error Detection
```typescript
// In the publish command, check npm exit code
const npmResult = await exec('npm publish', { cwd: pluginDir });
if (npmResult.exitCode !== 0) {
  throw new Error(`NPM publish failed: ${npmResult.stderr}`);
}
```

### 2. Add Package Scope Validation
```typescript
// Before publishing, check if scope exists
const scopeExists = await checkNpmScope('@elizaos');
if (!scopeExists) {
  throw new Error('Package scope @elizaos does not exist on npm. Contact npm support to create the scope.');
}
```

### 3. Add Post-Publish Validation
```typescript
// After publishing, verify package exists
const packageExists = await checkPackageExists(packageName, version);
if (!packageExists) {
  throw new Error(`Package was not published successfully. Check npm logs for details.`);
}
```

## Priority
**Critical** - This completely blocks plugin publishing and creates false success reports.

## Affected Components
- `packages/cli/src/commands/publish.ts`
- NPM publishing logic
- Error handling and success reporting
- Package scope validation
- Registry repository configuration

## Immediate Action Required
1. **Create the `@elizaos` scope on npm** or use an existing scope
2. Update CLI to properly detect and handle npm publish failures
3. Add post-publish validation to ensure packages actually exist on npm
4. Update registry repository configuration

## Alternative Solutions
1. **Use existing scope**: Change package name to `@monilpat/plugin-polygon`
2. **Create organization scope**: Set up `@elizaos` organization on npm
3. **Use unscoped package**: Publish as `elizaos-plugin-polygon`

This issue is critically blocking plugin developers from successfully publishing their work and needs immediate attention from the ElizaOS team.

root@DESKTOP-VJVJ4O4:/mnt/e/V.ON/plugin-delta#80:T1cfa,### **Ticket: Implement Run Orchestration & Isolation**

**ID:** `FEAT-126` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Scenario Matrix Runner`

**Tags:** `cli`, `scenario-testing`, `feature`, `orchestration`

**Estimated Story Points:** `13`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-123` (Matrix Configuration Schema), `FEAT-124` (CLI Command), `FEAT-125` (Parameter Override System)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Implement matrix run orchestration with complete isolation and cleanup`

#### **2. Description**

This ticket implements the core execution engine for the Scenario Matrix Runner. It orchestrates the execution of all matrix combinations, ensures complete isolation between runs, and manages cleanup of artifacts. This is the most complex ticket in Epic 1 as it ties together all previous work and handles the actual scenario execution.

The orchestration system must be robust, handling failures gracefully while maintaining data integrity and system cleanliness. Each run must be completely isolated to prevent interference between test executions.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **Matrix Execution Loop:**
   * Implement `executeMatrixRuns(config: MatrixConfig, combinations: MatrixCombination[]): Promise<MatrixRunResult[]>` that:
     * Iterates through all generated matrix combinations
     * Executes each combination the specified number of times (`runs_per_combination`)
     * Maintains execution order and provides progress feedback
     * Handles individual run failures without stopping the entire matrix execution
2. **Run Isolation System:**
   * Each scenario run must be completely isolated with:
     * Separate temporary directories for each run (e.g., `./temp/matrix-run-001/`)
     * Independent database instances (using unique database paths)
     * Isolated log files and output artifacts
     * Clean environment variable state between runs
   * Implement `createIsolatedEnvironment(runId: string): Promise<IsolationContext>` that sets up the isolated environment
   * Implement `cleanupIsolatedEnvironment(context: IsolationContext): Promise<void>` that removes all artifacts
3. **Scenario Override Integration:**
   * Use the parameter override system from ticket 1.3 to generate unique scenario configurations
   * Write the modified scenario to the isolated environment as a temporary `.scenario.yaml` file
   * Pass the temporary scenario file path to the existing scenario runner
4. **Progress Tracking and Logging:**
   * Real-time progress updates: `"Executing run 15 of 36 (Combination 5/12, Run 3/3)"`
   * Estimated time remaining based on average run duration
   * Clear indication of which parameter combination is currently running
   * Summary statistics after each combination completes
5. **Data Collection and Storage:**
   * Capture comprehensive data for each run:
     * Execution start and end timestamps
     * Parameter combination used
     * Scenario execution results (pass/fail, evaluation details)
     * Performance metrics (execution time, memory usage)
     * Any error messages or exceptions
   * Store results in structured JSON format in the output directory
   * Implement `saveRunResult(runId: string, result: ScenarioRunResult, outputDir: string): Promise<void>`
6. **Error Handling and Recovery:**
   * Graceful handling of individual run failures without stopping the matrix
   * Timeout handling for runs that exceed reasonable execution time
   * Resource cleanup even when runs fail or are interrupted
   * Detailed error logging with context about which combination failed
   * Option to continue or abort on first failure
7. **Resource Management:**
   * Monitor system resources (memory, disk space) during execution
   * Implement safeguards against resource exhaustion
   * Parallel execution limits to prevent system overload
   * Cleanup of temporary files and databases between runs
8. **Output Directory Structure:**

   ```
   output/matrix-<timestamp>/
   ├── config.yaml              # Copy of the matrix configuration
   ├── summary.json             # High-level execution summary
   ├── runs/
   │   ├── run-001.json         # Individual run results
   │   ├── run-002.json
   │   └── ...
   └── logs/
       ├── matrix-execution.log # Overall execution log
       ├── run-001.log         # Individual run logs
       └── ...
   ```

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**File Structure:**

```
packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/
├── matrix-orchestrator.ts     # Main orchestration logic
├── run-isolation.ts           # Environment isolation utilities
├── progress-tracker.ts        # Progress reporting and ETA calculation
├── resource-monitor.ts        # System resource monitoring
└── __tests__/
    ├── matrix-orchestrator.test.ts
    ├── run-isolation.test.ts
    └── integration/
        └── full-matrix.test.ts
```

```typescript
interface IsolationContext {
  runId: string;
  tempDir: string;
  dbPath: string;
  logPath: string;
  scenarioPath: string;
  cleanup: () => Promise<void>;
}
interface MatrixRunResult {
  runId: string;
  combinationId: string;
  parameters: Record<string, any>;
  startTime: Date;
  endTime: Date;
  duration: number;
  success: boolean;
  scenarioResult?: any;
  error?: string;
  metrics: {
    memoryUsage: number;
    diskUsage: number;
    tokenCount?: number;
  };
}
interface MatrixExecutionSummary {
  totalRuns: number;
  successfulRuns: number;
  failedRuns: number;
  totalDuration: number;
  averageRunTime: number;
  combinations: CombinationSummary[];
}
```

* Memory-efficient handling of large numbers of runs
