**Issue Title**: `fix(scenarios): Runtime dependency audit and fix for evaluators`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `bug`, `evaluators`, `runtime-integration`

#### **Description**

Several evaluators in the `EvaluationEngine` make assumptions about `IAgentRuntime` methods that may not exist or may have different signatures than expected. This creates a risk of runtime errors when scenarios are executed, and prevents certain evaluations from functioning correctly.

The issue stems from evaluators being implemented without first auditing the actual `IAgentRuntime` interface and available methods in the ElizaOS core.

#### **Specific Problem Methods**

**1.** `TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator`

```typescript
// Current problematic code in EvaluationEngine.ts
async evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, _result: ScenarioResult): Promise<boolean> {
    const memories = await runtime.getAllMemories(); // ❌ This method may not exist
    return memories.some((memory: any) => memory.content?.metadata?.action === this.action);
}
```

**2.** `LLMJudgeEvaluator`

```typescript
// Current problematic code in EvaluationEngine.ts
async evaluate(runtime: IAgentRuntime, result: ScenarioResult): Promise<boolean> {
    const llmResult = await runtime.useModel('TEXT_LARGE', { // ❌ Wrong method signature
        system: `...`,
        messages: [...]
    });
    return llmResult.toLowerCase().includes(this.expected.toLowerCase());
}
```

#### **Required Investigation and Fixes**

**1. Audit** `IAgentRuntime` Interface

Before making any changes, thoroughly examine the actual `IAgentRuntime` interface:

* **Review**: `packages/core/src/types.ts` or wherever `IAgentRuntime` is defined
* **Identify**: What methods are actually available for:
  * Accessing agent memory/action history
  * Making LLM calls
  * Querying agent state
* **Document**: Create a reference of available methods with their correct signatures

**2. Study Existing ElizaOS Patterns**

Examine how other parts of the codebase interact with the runtime:

* **Look at**: `packages/core/src/` - How do actions and providers access runtime capabilities?
* **Look at**: `packages/plugin-bootstrap/src/` - How do evaluators and actions query agent state?
* **Pattern**: Find examples of LLM calls, memory access, and action history retrieval
* **Follow**: Use the same patterns that exist elsewhere in the codebase

**3. Fix** `TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator`

* **Research**: How does ElizaOS actually store and retrieve action history?
* **Options to investigate**:
  * `runtime.messageManager` - for conversation/action logs
  * `runtime.databaseAdapter` - for direct database queries
  * Event/memory storage patterns used elsewhere
* **Implementation**: Use the correct ElizaOS pattern for accessing action trajectory

**Example research questions:**

```typescript
// What's the correct way to access action history?
// Is it one of these patterns?
const actionHistory = await runtime.messageManager.getMemories({...});
const events = await runtime.databaseAdapter.getEventHistory();
const memories = await runtime.memory.getMemoriesByType('action');
```

**4. Fix** `LLMJudgeEvaluator`

* **Research**: How do other parts of ElizaOS make LLM calls?
* **Find**: The correct method signature for LLM invocation
* **Pattern**: Look at how actions, providers, or other evaluators make LLM calls

**Example research questions:**

```typescript
// What's the correct way to call an LLM?
// Is it one of these patterns?
const response = await runtime.completion({...});
const result = await runtime.generateText({...});
const output = await runtime.llm.complete({...});
```

**5. Add Error Handling and Fallbacks**

For each fixed evaluator:

* **Graceful Degradation**: If a runtime method is unavailable, return a clear error or skip
* **Clear Error Messages**: Provide actionable error messages when dependencies are missing
* **Feature Detection**: Check if required runtime capabilities are available before using them

**6. Create Runtime Requirements Documentation**

* **Document**: What runtime capabilities each evaluator requires
* **Specify**: Minimum runtime interface requirements for scenarios
* **Guide**: Help future evaluator implementations avoid these issues

#### **Implementation Approach**

**Phase 1: Investigation (Do this first)**

1. Create a comprehensive audit of `IAgentRuntime` available methods
2. Find existing patterns in the codebase for:
   * LLM calls
   * Memory/action history access
   * Agent state queries
3. Document the correct patterns to follow

**Phase 2: Fix Implementation**

1. Update `TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator` to use correct action history access
2. Update `LLMJudgeEvaluator` to use correct LLM invocation method
3. Add proper error handling for missing capabilities
4. Update any other evaluators with similar issues

**Phase 3: Validation**

1. Test each evaluator with actual runtime instances
2. Verify error handling works correctly
3. Confirm evaluators integrate properly with the rest of the scenario system

#### **Testing Strategy**

**1. Runtime Method Validation**

* Create test scenarios that exercise each evaluator type
* Verify no runtime errors occur during evaluation execution
* Test both success and failure cases for each evaluator

**2. Integration Testing**

* Test evaluators with actual agent runtimes
* Verify trajectory evaluation works with real action sequences
* Verify LLM judge works with actual model calls

**3. Error Handling**

* Test evaluators with mock runtimes that don't have required methods
* Verify graceful error handling and clear error messages

#### **Success Criteria**

1. All evaluators use correct `IAgentRuntime` methods with proper signatures
2. No runtime errors occur when evaluators are executed
3. `TrajectoryContainsActionEvaluator` correctly identifies actions in agent execution history
4. `LLMJudgeEvaluator` successfully makes LLM calls and processes responses
5. Clear error messages when runtime capabilities are missing
6. Documentation exists for runtime requirements of each evaluator

#### **Implementation Guidelines**

**⚠️ Critical Research First:**

1. **Don't assume method signatures** - Always verify against actual `IAgentRuntime` interface
2. **Follow existing patterns** - Find how other ElizaOS components do LLM calls and memory access
3. **Study plugin-bootstrap** - This likely contains examples of evaluators that work correctly
4. **Test incrementally** - Fix one evaluator at a time and verify it works
5. **Document findings** - Create clear documentation of correct patterns for future reference

This fix ensures the evaluation engine works reliably with actual ElizaOS runtime instances and follows established patterns in the codebase.82:T1a01,### 

**Issue Title**: `fix(scenarios): Fix environment provider integration - bypass issue`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `bug`, `critical`, `environment-provider`

#### **Description**

The scenario runner currently bypasses the sophisticated environment provider system entirely. The `handleRunScenario` function directly executes shell commands using `child_process.exec` instead of calling the properly implemented `LocalEnvironmentProvider` and `E2BEnvironmentProvider` classes. This means that virtual file systems, proper sandboxing, and environment-specific logic are not functioning as intended.

This is a **critical bug** that breaks core functionality and prevents the scenario system from working as designed.

#### **Current Implementation Problem**

In `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`, the `handleRunScenario` function currently does this:

```typescript
async function handleRunScenario(_args: {filePath: string, live: boolean}, _runtime: any, scenario: any): Promise<ScenarioResult> {
    const runStep = scenario.run[0];
    
    return new Promise<ScenarioResult>((resolve) => {
        exec(runStep.input, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
            resolve({ stdout, stderr, exitCode: error ? error.code || 1 : 0 });
        });
    });
}
```

**This completely ignores the environment providers that were carefully implemented.**

#### **Expected Implementation**

The function should instantiate and use the appropriate environment provider based on `scenario.environment.type`:

```typescript
async function handleRunScenario(args: {filePath: string, live: boolean}, runtime: IAgentRuntime, scenario: Scenario): Promise<ScenarioResult> {
    let provider: EnvironmentProvider;
    
    // Instantiate the correct provider
    if (scenario.environment.type === 'e2b') {
        provider = new E2BEnvironmentProvider(runtime);
    } else if (scenario.environment.type === 'local') {
        provider = new LocalEnvironmentProvider();
    } else {
        throw new Error(`Unsupported environment type: ${scenario.environment.type}`);
    }
    
    try {
        await provider.setup(scenario);
        return await provider.run(scenario);
    } finally {
        await provider.teardown();
    }
}
```

#### **Root Cause Analysis**

Based on the AI implementation feedback, this likely happened because:

1. The AI didn't review existing ElizaOS patterns for environment abstraction
2. The AI took a shortcuts approach instead of following the designed architecture
3. The ticket didn't emphasize examining existing provider implementations in the codebase

#### **Required Changes**

**1. Fix the Main Handler Function**

* **Modify**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts` - `handleRunScenario` function
* **Change**: Remove direct `child_process.exec` usage
* **Add**: Proper environment provider instantiation and lifecycle management

**2. Review Existing ElizaOS Environment Patterns**

Before implementing, examine these existing patterns in the codebase:

* **Look at**: `packages/cli/src/commands/start/` - How does the start command manage different environments?
* **Look at**: `packages/core/src/` - Are there existing environment abstraction patterns?
* **Look at**: Other plugins that manage execution environments
* **Pattern**: Follow existing ElizaOS conventions for service instantiation and lifecycle

**3. E2B Integration Specifics**

Based on the [`@elizaos/plugin-e2b` documentation](https://github.com/elizaos-plugins/plugin-e2b), ensure proper integration:

* **Service Discovery**: Use `runtime.getService('e2b')` to get the E2B service
* **Error Handling**: Implement the recommended error checking pattern from the plugin docs
* **API Usage**: Use the correct E2B service methods:
  * `createSandbox()` for sandbox creation
  * `executeCode()` for command execution (not `runCommand()` as currently implemented)
  * `writeFileToSandbox()` for virtual file system setup
  * `killSandbox()` for cleanup
* **Configuration**: Respect E2B environment variables (`E2B_API_KEY`, `E2B_MODE`)

**4. Local Environment Integration**

* **Working Directory**: Ensure commands execute in the temporary directory created by `LocalEnvironmentProvider.setup()`
* **File System**: Verify that `virtual_fs` files are actually available to the executed commands
* **Cleanup**: Ensure temporary directories are properly cleaned up even on errors

**5. Error Handling & Integration**

* **Provider Errors**: Catch and properly format provider-specific errors
* **Service Availability**: Handle cases where E2B service is not available with clear error messages
* **Graceful Degradation**: Implement proper fallback behavior when providers fail

#### **Testing Strategy**

**1. Verify Virtual File System Works**

* Test scenario with `setup.virtual_fs` containing test files
* Verify commands can read and modify those files
* Confirm files are properly isolated between test runs

**2. Test E2B Integration**

* Create test scenario that requires E2B (`environment: { type: 'e2b' }`)
* Verify sandbox creation, file writing, and command execution
* Test both with and without `E2B_API_KEY` to verify error handling

**3. Test Local Environment**

* Verify local scenarios work with temporary directory isolation
* Test complex scenarios with multiple commands and file operations

**4. Error Scenarios**

* Test with invalid environment types
* Test E2B scenarios without the plugin installed
* Test scenarios that fail during setup or execution

#### **Implementation Notes**

**⚠️ Critical Implementation Guidelines:**

1. **Review Existing Code First**: Before writing any new code, examine how other parts of ElizaOS handle environment abstraction and service integration
2. **Follow ElizaOS Patterns**: Use the same patterns for service discovery, error handling, and lifecycle management that exist elsewhere in the codebase
3. **E2B Plugin Integration**: Study the actual E2B plugin implementation to understand the correct service interface, don't assume the interface based on the provider code alone
4. **Test Incrementally**: Fix local environment first (simpler), then E2B integration
5. **Preserve Existing Functionality**: Ensure the mock engine, evaluation engine, and reporting continue to work exactly as they do now

**Dependencies:**

* The `LocalEnvironmentProvider` and `E2BEnvironmentProvider` classes are correctly implemented
* The `@elizaos/plugin-e2b` service interface matches what's expected
* The scenario schema and validation logic remain unchanged

**ID:** `FEAT-133` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Performance Reporting Dashboard`

**Tags:** `cli`, `reporting`, `feature`, `javascript`, `visualization`

**Estimated Story Points:** `8`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-131` (Data Aggregation), `FEAT-132` (HTML Template)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Implement dynamic data injection and rendering for the HTML Performance Report`

#### **2. Description**

This ticket brings the Performance Report to life. It involves creating the logic to take the aggregated `report.json` data and inject it into the static `report_template.html`, producing a final, fully-rendered, and interactive HTML file.

The work will primarily be in modifying the `elizaos report generate` command to read both the data and the template, inject the data, and write the final output. This includes writing the client-side JavaScript (embedded within the template) that will be responsible for all DOM manipulation, chart generation, and rendering of dynamic tables and lists.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **File I/O in** `generate` command:
   * The `elizaos report generate` command is updated to:
     * Read the aggregated `report.json` file into memory.
     * Read the static `report_template.html` file into memory.
     * Inject the entire JSON data object into the `<script id="report-data">` tag within the HTML.
     * Save the resulting string as the final report file (e.g., `performance_report.html`).
2. **Client-Side Rendering Logic:**
   * The JavaScript embedded in the `report_template.html` is implemented.
   * On `DOMContentLoaded`, the script must:
     * Parse the JSON from the `<script id="report-data">` tag.
     * Call a main `renderReport(data)` function with the parsed data.
3. **DOM Population:**
   * The `renderReport` function correctly populates all the simple data placeholders defined in the template (e.g., setting the `innerText` of `<span id="summary-total-runs">`).
4. **Chart Generation:**
   * The script uses the embedded Chart.js library to render meaningful visualizations:
     * A **Bar Chart** for "Capability Success Rates," showing the success percentage for each capability.
     * A **Grouped Bar Chart** for "Results by Parameter," allowing for easy comparison of key metrics (like success rate and average execution time) across different parameter values (e.g., comparing `gpt-4` vs. `gpt-3.5`).
   * Charts must be correctly labeled, include tooltips, and use colors that are consistent with the report's design.
5. **Dynamic Table Rendering:**
   * The "Detailed Run Explorer" table is dynamically populated by creating `<tr>` elements for each run in the `raw_results` array and appending them to the `<tbody id="detailed-runs-tbody">`.
   * The table should be searchable and sortable (using a lightweight, embedded library or custom JavaScript).
6. **Trajectory Visualization:**
   * The "Common Action Trajectories" section is populated.
   * This includes logic to render the aggregated trajectory data, potentially as a simple ranked list or as a more advanced visual element like a Sankey diagram (using a library like D3 or Google Charts, if feasible to embed).

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify:**

* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/generate.ts`: Add the file I/O and data injection logic.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/src/assets/report_template.html`: Implement the client-side JavaScript rendering logic within the `<script>` tags.

**Example Client-Side JavaScript Snippet:**

```html
<script>
  document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
    const dataElement = document.getElementById('report-data');
    if (dataElement) {
      const reportData = JSON.parse(dataElement.textContent);
      renderReport(reportData);
    }
  });
  function renderReport(data) {
    // 1. Populate simple values
    document.getElementById('summary-total-runs').innerText = data.summary_stats.total_runs;
    
    // 2. Render Capability Chart
    const ctx = document.getElementById('capability-chart').getContext('2d');
    new Chart(ctx, {
      type: 'bar',
      data: {
        labels: Object.keys(data.summary_stats.capability_success_rates),
        datasets: [{
          label: 'Success Rate',
          data: Object.values(data.summary_stats.capability_success_rates).map(d => d * 100),
          // ...chart options
        }]
      }
    });
    // 3. Render Detailed Runs Table
    const tbody = document.getElementById('detailed-runs-tbody');
    data.raw_results.forEach(run => {
      const row = document.createElement('tr');
      // ...create and append cells for the run data
      tbody.appendChild(row);
    });
  }
</script>
```

The goal is to create a professional, clean, and data-rich HTML file that will serve as the template for our reporting system. This template will be a static asset, later populated with dynamic data by the report generation logic.

The design should prioritize clarity and ease of navigation, allowing a developer to quickly understand the high-level results of a matrix run and then drill down into specific areas of interest. The final deliverable is a single, self-contained `.html` file with embedded CSS and JavaScript, ensuring the report is easily shareable and viewable in any modern web browser without requiring a web server.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **Report Structure and Layout:**
   * A clear, hierarchical layout is established for the report. The design must include distinct sections for:
     * **Report Header:** Title, date of generation, and a link to the matrix configuration used.
     * **High-Level Summary:** Key metrics displayed prominently at the top (e.g., total runs, overall success rate, average execution time).
     * **Results by Parameter:** A section for each matrix parameter (e.g., "Performance by LLM Model"), with subsections for each value.
     * **Capability Analysis:** A dedicated section showing the success rate for each defined agent capability.
     * **Trajectory Analysis:** A section to visualize the most common action sequences.
     * **Detailed Run Explorer:** A table or list view to browse the raw data for every individual run.
2. **Visual Design and Styling:**

   * Styling is embedded directly within the HTML file in a `<style>` tag to ensure portability.
   * The design must be responsive and readable on both desktop and mobile screen sizes.
   * Use of color should be intentional, highlighting key data points (e.g., green for success, red for failure) while remaining accessible.
3. **Component Placeholders:**
   * The HTML template must contain clearly identifiable placeholders for all dynamic data. This includes:
     * `<span>` or `<div>` elements with specific `id` attributes for single data points (e.g., `<span id="total-runs"></span>`).
     * HTML `<canvas>` elements for charts, each with a unique `id`.
     * Template blocks or empty table bodies (`<tbody>`) for data that will be rendered in loops (e.g., the detailed run list).
4. **JavaScript Integration:**
   * The chosen charting library ([Chart.js](https://www.chartjs.org/) is recommended) is embedded directly into the HTML file in a `<script>` tag.
   * A second embedded `<script>` tag will contain the "rendering" logic. This script will:
     * Define a function like `renderReport(data)`, where `data` is the `ReportData` JSON object.
     * This function will contain the logic to find the placeholders in the DOM and populate them with the data.
     * A placeholder for the data itself is included, e.g., `<script id="report-data" type="application/json">...</script>`.
5. **Self-Contained Asset:**
   * The final deliverable is a single `.html` file. All CSS and JavaScript must be embedded. No external network requests should be necessary to view the report's structure and styling (data will be injected later).

**ID:** `FEAT-131` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Performance Reporting Dashboard`

**Tags:** `cli`, `reporting`, `feature`, `data-analysis`

**Estimated Story Points:** `8`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-130` (Centralize and Serialize Run Data)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Implement 'elizaos report generate' command for data aggregation and analysis`

#### **2. Description**

This ticket introduces the user-facing entry point for the Performance Reporting Dashboard: the `elizaos report generate` command. The primary responsibility of this command is to ingest the raw JSON output from a Scenario Matrix run, process it, and perform the complex data aggregation required to generate an insightful report.

The command will read all individual `run-*.json` files, calculate high-level statistics, group results by the matrix parameters, and analyze agent trajectories. The final output of this command will be a single, structured `ReportData` object, which will serve as the complete data context for rendering the HTML report in a subsequent ticket.

This is a critical data processing step that transforms raw run data into meaningful, aggregated insights.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **CLI Command Registration:**
   * A new top-level command `report` is created.
   * Under `report`, a subcommand `generate` is registered.
   * The command accepts one required argument, `<input_dir>`, which is the path to the output directory of a matrix run.
   * It also accepts an optional `--output-path` flag to specify where to save the final `report.json` file. If not provided, it defaults to `<input_dir>/report.json`.
2. **Data Ingestion and Validation:**
   * The command recursively finds and reads all `run-*.json` files within the `<input_dir>`.
   * It validates the structure of each JSON file against the `ScenarioRunResult` schema.
   * Malformed or incomplete files are gracefully skipped, and a warning is logged.
   * The command provides a clear error and exits if the input directory is not found or contains no valid run files.
3. **Data Aggregation Logic:**
   * The core of the command is an `AnalysisEngine` that processes the array of `ScenarioRunResult` objects.
   * It must calculate **overall summary statistics**: total runs, average execution time, overall capability success rates, etc.
   * It must calculate **grouped statistics**: The engine must group the results by each matrix parameter and value (e.g., group by `character.llm.model`). For each group, it calculates the same summary statistics, allowing for direct comparison (e.g., success rate of `gpt-4` vs. `gpt-3.5`).
   * It must perform **trajectory analysis**: Aggregate all `trajectory` arrays to identify the most common action sequences and calculate the frequency of each path.
4. **Structured** `ReportData` Output:
   * The `AnalysisEngine` produces a single, large `ReportData` object.
   * This object's schema is formally defined in TypeScript and contains all aggregated data in a clean, predictable structure, ready for a rendering engine.
   * The command serializes this `ReportData` object to a pretty-printed JSON file at the specified output path.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**File Structure:**

```
packages/cli/src/commands/report/
├── index.ts              # Registers the 'report' command
├── generate.ts           # Implements the 'generate' subcommand
└── src/
    ├── analysis-engine.ts  # Core data aggregation logic
    ├── report-schema.ts    # Defines the 'ReportData' interface
    └── __tests__/
        └── analysis-engine.test.ts
```

`ReportData` Interface (High-Level Example):

```typescript
interface ReportData {
  metadata: {
    report_generated_at: string;
    matrix_config: MatrixConfig; // From the original run
  };
  summary_stats: {
    total_runs: number;
    total_failed_runs: number;
    average_execution_time: number;
    capability_success_rates: Record<string, number>; // { "Formats Response": 0.75 }
  };
  results_by_parameter: {
    [parameter_name: string]: { // e.g., "character.llm.model"
      [parameter_value: string]: ReportSummaryStats; // e.g., "gpt-4-turbo" has its own summary_stats
    };
  };
  common_trajectories: {
    sequence: string[]; // e.g., ["THINK", "LIST_ISSUES", "REPLY"]
    count: number;
    average_duration: number;
  }[];
  raw_results: ScenarioRunResult[]; // Include all original data for detailed drill-downs
}
```

**Example CLI Usage:**

```bash
# Generate a report from a previous matrix run
elizaos report generate ./output/matrix-20231027-1000/
# Specify a different output location for the aggregated data
elizaos report generate ./output/matrix-20231027-1000/ --output-path ./reports/latest-report.json
```

#### **5. Testing Requirements**

* **Unit Tests:**
  * Extensive unit tests for the `AnalysisEngine` are critical. Provide it with a mock array of `ScenarioRunResult` objects and assert that the resulting `ReportData` object contains the correct aggregations, groupings, and statistical calculations.
  * Test edge cases like empty input, single-run input, and runs with missing data points.

  * Create a test that runs the `elizaos report generate` command on a pre-generated fixture directory containing a set of `run-*.json` files.
  * The test should verify that the command exits successfully and that the output `report.json` file is created and contains valid, non-empty data.

#### **6. Out of Scope**

* The design or implementation of the final HTML template. This ticket's final artifact is the `report.json` file, not a user-facing document.
* The logic for rendering charts or any other UI components (handled in Ticket 3.2 and 3.3).
* Any modification to the scenario running or data collection process; this command is strictly read-only on the matrix run output.87:T17e7,### **Ticket: Centralize and Serialize Run Data**

**ID:** `FEAT-130` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Advanced Evaluation & Data Collection`

**Tags:** `cli`, `scenario-testing`, `feature`, `data-pipeline`

**Estimated Story Points:** `5`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-126` (Run Orchestration), `FEAT-127` (Structured JSON Output), `FEAT-129` (Agent Trajectory Logging)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Centralize and serialize all scenario run data into a structured JSON output file`

#### **2. Description**

This ticket focuses on the final step of the data collection phase: bringing together all the rich data gathered from a single scenario run and saving it to a well-defined, structured JSON file. This file will be the canonical source of truth for a single execution and will serve as the input for the Performance Reporting Dashboard (Epic 3).

The implementation will create a new service or utility responsible for aggregating data from various sources—the matrix configuration, the evaluation engine, the agent runtime's trajectory log, and performance timers—and ensuring it conforms to our master `ScenarioRunResult` schema before being written to disk.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. `ScenarioRunResult` Schema:
   * A comprehensive `ScenarioRunResult` interface is finalized in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`.
   * This master interface must consolidate all data points from the previous tickets and include:
     * `run_id`: A unique identifier for the run.
     * `matrix_combination_id`: An identifier linking it to a specific set of parameters.
     * `parameters`: An object detailing the specific matrix configuration for this run (e.g., `{ "character.llm.model": "gpt-4-turbo" }`).
     * `metrics`: An object containing performance data (`execution_time_seconds`, `llm_calls`, `total_tokens`, etc.).
     * `evaluations`: The array of `EvaluationResult` objects from the `EvaluationEngine` (Ticket 2.1).
     * `trajectory`: The array of `TrajectoryStep` objects from the `AgentRuntime` (Ticket 2.3).
     * `final_agent_response`: The final text/object response from the agent to the user.
     * `error`: A field to store any fatal error message if the run failed unexpectedly.
2. **Data Aggregation Utility:**
   * A new utility or class, e.g., `RunDataAggregator`, is created.
   * It will have methods to collect data from different parts of the system throughout a run's lifecycle.
   * It will have a final `buildResult()` method that assembles all the collected pieces into a single, validated `ScenarioRunResult` object.
3. **File Serialization Logic:**
   * The `MatrixOrchestrator` (from Ticket 1.4) will use this new utility to generate the result for each run.
   * After a run completes (or fails), the orchestrator will call a function to serialize the `ScenarioRunResult` object to a JSON file.
   * The output file will be named according to a consistent pattern (e.g., `run-<run_id>.json`) and saved within the unique output directory created for the matrix execution.
   * The JSON output must be cleanly formatted and human-readable (pretty-printed with an indentation of 2 spaces).
4. **Integration with Matrix Orchestrator:**
   * The main execution loop in the `MatrixOrchestrator` is updated to wrap each scenario run in a `try...catch` block.
   * In both success and failure cases, the orchestrator ensures that the data aggregator is called and a result file is written, capturing the error details if the run failed.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify / Create:**

* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`: Finalize the `ScenarioRunResult` master interface.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/data-aggregator.ts`: New file for the `RunDataAggregator` class.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/matrix-orchestrator.ts`: Integrate the aggregator and serialization logic into the main execution loop.

**Final** `ScenarioRunResult` JSON Structure (Example):

```json
{
  "run_id": "run-20231027-015",
  "matrix_combination_id": "combo-003",
  "parameters": {
    "character.llm.model": "gpt-4-turbo",
    "run[0].input": "Show me what's open in the elizaOS/eliza GitHub."
  },
  "metrics": {
    "execution_time_seconds": 14.7,
    "llm_calls": 2,
    "total_tokens": 2100
  },
  "final_agent_response": "Here are the open issues for elizaOS/eliza: #123 Fix the login button, #124 Improve documentation...",
  "evaluations": [
    {
      "evaluator_type": "llm_judge",
      "success": false,
      "summary": "...",
      "details": {
        "qualitative_summary": "...",
        "capability_checklist": [
          { "capability": "Formats Final Response", "achieved": false, "reasoning": "..." }
        ]
      }
    }
  ],
  "trajectory": [
    { "type": "thought", "content": "..." },
    { "type": "action", "content": { "name": "LIST_GITHUB_ISSUES", "parameters": { "owner": "elizaOS", "repo": "eliza" } } },
    { "type": "observation", "content": "[...]" },
    { "type": "thought", "content": "..." }
  ],
  "error": null
}
```

#### **5. Testing Requirements**

* **Unit Tests:**
  * Test the `RunDataAggregator` to ensure it correctly assembles the `ScenarioRunResult` object from mock inputs.
  * Test the file serialization logic, including pretty-printing and correct file pathing.
  * Test the error handling path, ensuring that a valid result file (with the `error` field populated) is created even when a run fails catastrophically.
* **Integration Tests:**
  * Run a full matrix test with a small number of combinations.
  * After the test completes, manually inspect the generated JSON files in the output directory to verify their structure and content are correct and complete.

#### **6. Out of Scope**

* The creation of the final `summary.json` file for the entire matrix run. This ticket is only concerned with the individual `run-*.json` files.
* Any form of report generation or data visualization (this is handled in Epic 3).
* The implementation of any of the data sources themselves (e.g., trajectory logging); this ticket only consumes and aggregates the data.88:T15cc,### **Ticket: Implement Agent Trajectory Logging**

**ID:** `FEAT-129` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Advanced Evaluation & Data Collection`

**Tags:** `core`, `agent-runtime`, `scenario-testing`, `feature`, `instrumentation`

**Estimated Story Points:** `5`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-127` (Structured JSON Output)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(core): Instrument AgentRuntime to capture and expose agent's cognitive trajectory`

#### **2. Description**

To effectively analyze and debug an agent's behavior—especially for complex tasks involving action chaining—we need to record its step-by-step cognitive process. This "trajectory" consists of the agent's reasoning (thoughts), the actions it takes, and the results of those actions (observations).

This ticket covers the work to instrument the core `AgentRuntime` to capture this sequence of events during the processing of a single user message. The captured trajectory will then be exposed to the Scenario Runner, making it a critical data source for the `LLMJudge` and the final performance reports.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. `TrajectoryStep` Interface Definition:
   * A new set of interfaces is defined in `packages/core/src/types.ts` to represent the trajectory.
   * `TrajectoryStep`: A union type representing a single step, containing `type`, `timestamp`, and `content`.
   * `ThoughtStep`: `type: 'thought'`, `content: string` (The LLM's reasoning before taking an action).
   * `ActionStep`: `type: 'action'`, `content: { name: string, parameters: object }` (The action and its arguments).
   * `ObservationStep`: `type: 'observation'`, `content: any` (The result returned from the action).
2. `AgentRuntime` Instrumentation:
   * The `AgentRuntime` is modified to maintain an internal, ordered log of `TrajectoryStep` objects for the duration of a single `handleMessage` or equivalent processing cycle.
   * Instrumentation points are added to capture:
     * The `thought` generated by the LLM.
     * The `action` call, including its name and parameters, immediately before execution.
     * The `observation` (the return value or error) immediately after the action completes.
   * The trajectory log must be cleared at the beginning of each new message processing cycle to ensure logs don't mix.
3. **Trajectory Data Exposure:**
   * The `AgentRuntime` must provide a mechanism to retrieve the trajectory for the most recently processed message. This could be a new method like `getLatestTrajectory(): TrajectoryStep[]`.
   * The Scenario Runner is updated to call this method after the agent generates its response.
4. **Integration with Data Collection:**
   * The collected `TrajectoryStep[]` array is added to the final structured JSON output for each scenario run.
   * A new top-level key, `trajectory`, will be added to the run result schema defined in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify:**

* `packages/core/src/runtime.ts`: The main location for instrumentation. The `private trajectory: TrajectoryStep[]` property will be added, and hooks will be placed within the core action/decision loop.
* `packages/core/src/types.ts`: Add the new `TrajectoryStep` related interfaces.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/EvaluationEngine.ts` (or the main runner logic): Update to fetch the trajectory from the runtime after a run.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`: Add the `trajectory` field to the main run result interface.

**Example** `trajectory` array in the final JSON output:

```json
"trajectory": [
  {
    "type": "thought",
    "timestamp": "2023-10-27T10:00:01Z",
    "content": "The user wants to know the open issues for a GitHub repository. I need to use the GitHub plugin to get this information. The repository is 'elizaOS/eliza'."
  },
  {
    "type": "action",
    "timestamp": "2023-10-27T10:00:02Z",
    "content": {
      "name": "LIST_GITHUB_ISSUES",
      "parameters": {
        "owner": "elizaOS",
        "repo": "eliza"
      }
    }
  },
  {
    "type": "observation",
    "timestamp": "2023-10-27T10:00:04Z",
    "content": [
      { "id": 123, "title": "Fix the login button", "state": "open" },
      { "id": 124, "title": "Improve documentation for scenarios", "state": "open" }
    ]
  },
  {
    "type": "thought",
    "timestamp": "2023-10-27T10:00:05Z",
    "content": "I have the list of issues. Now I need to format this information into a clear, readable list and reply to the user."
  }
]
```

#### **5. Testing Requirements**

* **Unit Tests:**
  * Add tests to `AgentRuntime` to verify that the trajectory is correctly captured for a single action.
  * Test that the trajectory is correctly captured for a sequence of multiple actions (if the agent supports multi-step execution).
  * Test that the trajectory log is cleared properly between separate message handling calls.
  * Test the case where an action throws an error, ensuring the `observation` step correctly records the error object.
* **Integration Tests:**
  * Run an existing scenario (like `test-github-issues.scenario.yaml`).
  * After the run completes, parse the output JSON file and assert that the `trajectory` field exists and contains a logically correct sequence of steps.

#### **6. Out of Scope**

* The use of the trajectory data by any evaluator, including the `LLMJudge`. This ticket's responsibility ends once the data is successfully captured and saved in the run result file.
* The visualization or rendering of the trajectory in the final HTML report. This is part of Epic 3.89:T134b,### **Ticket: Implement Scenario Capability Definitions**

**ID:** `FEAT-128` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Advanced Evaluation & Data Collection`

**Tags:** `scenario-testing`, `evaluation`, `llm`, `feature`

**Estimated Story Points:** `3`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-127` (Structured JSON Output)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Allow defining a 'capabilities' checklist in scenario YAML for LLM Judge`

#### **2. Description**

To make the `LLMJudge` evaluator more flexible and scenario-specific, we need to allow developers to define the exact set of "capabilities" that should be assessed for each test. Currently, these capabilities are hardcoded in the LLM's prompt.

This ticket involves extending the `.scenario.yaml` schema to accept a new `capabilities` array within an `llm_judge` evaluation. This list will be dynamically injected into the `LLMJudge`'s prompt, instructing it to evaluate the agent's performance against this specific, user-defined checklist.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **Schema Extension:**
   * The `.scenario.yaml` schema is updated to allow a `capabilities` key for any evaluation of `type: "llm_judge"`.
   * The `capabilities` key must be an array of strings. Each string represents a specific capability the agent should be evaluated against (e.g., "Correctly identifies the user's core request").
   * Validation logic (from Ticket 1.1) is updated to enforce that `capabilities` is a non-empty array of strings if provided.
2. **TypeScript Interface Update:**
   * The TypeScript interfaces in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts` are updated to reflect the new `capabilities` field on the `LLMJudgeEvaluation` type.
3. **Dynamic Prompt Injection:**
   * The `LLMJudge` evaluator logic is modified to detect if a `capabilities` array is present in its configuration.
   * If present, the evaluator must dynamically construct a portion of its prompt to the LLM, instructing it to use the provided list as the basis for its `capability_checklist` output. The prompt should clearly list each capability it needs to assess.
   * If the `capabilities` array is not provided, the `LLMJudge` should fall back to a default, generic set of capabilities to ensure backward compatibility.
4. **Integration with EvaluationEngine:**
   * The `EvaluationEngine` must correctly parse the `capabilities` from the scenario file and pass them into the `LLMJudge` evaluator during initialization.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify:**

* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`: Add `capabilities?: string[]` to the `LLMJudgeEvaluation` interface and update Zod schema.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/evaluators/llm_judge.ts`: Modify the evaluator to accept the `capabilities` array and dynamically build the prompt.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/prompts/llm_judge_structured.prompt.txt`: Update the prompt template to include a placeholder for the dynamic capabilities list.

**Example** `.scenario.yaml` Configuration:

```yaml
# ... (scenario setup)
run:
  - input: "List open issues for elizaOS/eliza and summarize them."
    evaluations:
      - type: "llm_judge"
        # The new capabilities block
        capabilities:
          - "Understands the multi-step nature of the request (list AND summarize)"
          - "Successfully retrieves the list of GitHub issues"
          - "Provides an accurate and concise summary of the issues"
          - "Formats the final response in a clean, readable manner"
```

**Example Dynamic Prompt Fragment:**

```
...
You must evaluate the agent's performance by filling out a JSON object. For the 'capability_checklist' field, you will assess the agent on the following specific capabilities:
1.  Understands the multi-step nature of the request (list AND summarize)
2.  Successfully retrieves the list of GitHub issues
3.  Provides an accurate and concise summary of the issues
4.  Formats the final response in a clean, readable manner
For each capability, you must provide a boolean 'achieved' status and a brief 'reasoning'.
...
```

#### **5. Testing Requirements**

* **Unit Tests:**
  * Create a unit test for the `LLMJudge` evaluator that provides a mock `capabilities` list and asserts that the generated prompt correctly includes them.
  * Add a test case where `capabilities` are not provided to ensure the fallback to default capabilities works correctly.
* **Integration Tests:**
  * Create a new example scenario file (`llm-judge-with-capabilities.scenario.yaml`) that uses the new feature.
  * Run an integration test with this scenario to ensure the end-to-end flow works as expected.

#### **6. Out of Scope**

* The creation of a UI or other mechanism for discovering or suggesting common capabilities. For now, they are manually defined by the developer writing the scenario.
* Complex validation of the capability strings themselves (e.g., checking for length or content).8a:T1898,### 

**ID:** `FEAT-127` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Advanced Evaluation & Data Collection`

**Tags:** `scenario-testing`, `evaluation`, `llm`, `reporting`, `refactor`

**Estimated Story Points:** `8`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-126` (Run Orchestration)

#### **1. Title**

`refactor(cli): Enhance EvaluationEngine to produce structured JSON output instead of booleans`

#### **2. Description**

The current scenario evaluation system returns a simple boolean (`pass`/`fail`), which is insufficient for our new, detailed reporting framework. This ticket involves a significant refactor of the `EvaluationEngine` and all individual evaluators to move from a binary result to a rich, structured JSON object.

This change is critical for capturing nuanced performance data. The most substantial part of this work will be enhancing the `LLMJudge` evaluator to perform a qualitative analysis and return a structured assessment, including a summary and a checklist of capabilities.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **Standardized** `EvaluationResult` Interface:
   * A new interface, `EvaluationResult`, is defined in `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts`.
   * This interface must include at least the following fields:
     * `evaluator_type`: (string) The name of the evaluator (e.g., `string_contains`, `llm_judge`).
     * `success`: (boolean) The final pass/fail outcome for this specific evaluation.
     * `summary`: (string) A one-line, human-readable summary of the result (e.g., "Assertion PASSED: Agent response contained the substring 'help'.").
     * `details`: (object) A flexible object containing evaluator-specific data (e.g., the substring searched for, the actual text found, etc.).
2. **Refactor** `EvaluationEngine`:
   * The `EvaluationEngine.run()` method is updated to return `Promise<EvaluationResult[]>`.
   * It no longer calculates a single boolean result but instead aggregates the structured `EvaluationResult` objects from all the evaluations it runs.
3. **Update Standard Evaluators:**
   * All existing, non-LLM evaluators (e.g., `string_contains`, `file_exists`, `trajectory_contains_action`) are refactored to return a `Promise<EvaluationResult>`.
   * The `details` object for each result must be populated with relevant context. For example, `string_contains` should include the `expected_value` and the `actual_output` in its details.
4. **Enhance** `LLMJudge` for Structured Output:
   * A new, sophisticated prompt is engineered for the `LLMJudge`. This prompt will instruct the LLM to act as an impartial evaluator and return a JSON object.
   * The LLM-generated JSON must conform to a new `LLMJudgeResult` interface, which includes:
     * `qualitative_summary`: (string) A concise, natural-language paragraph summarizing the agent's performance, reasoning, and any notable successes or failures.
     * `capability_checklist`: (array) An array of objects, where each object represents a specific capability and contains:
       * `capability`: (string) The name of the capability being assessed.
       * `achieved`: (boolean) Whether the agent successfully demonstrated this capability.
       * `reasoning`: (string) A brief explanation from the LLM justifying its assessment for that capability.
   * The `LLMJudge` evaluator implementation must:
     * Reliably parse the JSON output from the LLM.
     * Implement robust error handling and retry logic for cases where the LLM returns malformed or invalid JSON.
     * Populate its `EvaluationResult.details` field with the parsed `LLMJudgeResult` object.
5. **Strong Typing:**
   * All new data structures (`EvaluationResult`, `LLMJudgeResult`, `CapabilityCheck`) must have corresponding TypeScript interfaces in the schema file to ensure type safety.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify / Create:**

* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/EvaluationEngine.ts` (Major refactor)
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/evaluators/*.ts` (Refactor all existing evaluator files)
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/schema.ts` (Add new interfaces)
* `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/prompts/llm_judge_structured.prompt.txt` (New file for the enhanced prompt)

**Example** `EvaluationResult` for `string_contains`:

```json
{
  "evaluator_type": "string_contains",
  "success": true,
  "summary": "Assertion PASSED: Agent response contained the expected substring.",
  "details": {
    "expected_value": "help",
    "actual_output": "Hello! I'm here to help you..."
  }
}
```

**Example** `EvaluationResult` for `llm_judge`:

```json
{
  "evaluator_type": "llm_judge",
  "success": false,
  "summary": "LLM Judge determined the agent failed to format its response correctly.",
  "details": {
    "qualitative_summary": "The agent correctly identified the user's intent... however, it failed to format the output into a readable list.",
    "capability_checklist": [
      { "capability": "Understands User Intent", "achieved": true, "reasoning": "..." },
      { "capability": "Selects Correct Tool", "achieved": true, "reasoning": "..." },
      { "capability": "Formats Final Response", "achieved": false, "reasoning": "The final reply was a single block of text, not a list." }
    ]
  }
}
```

#### **5. Testing Requirements**

* **Unit Tests:**
  * Update all existing evaluator tests to assert against the new `EvaluationResult` structure.
  * Create new tests for the `LLMJudge` to verify its prompt engineering and JSON parsing capabilities. This should include mocking both valid and malformed JSON responses from the LLM to test the retry and error handling logic.
* **Integration Tests:**
  * Update the main `EvaluationEngine` tests to confirm that it correctly aggregates structured results from a mix of different evaluator types.

#### **6. Out of Scope**

* Allowing users to define the list of `capabilities` in the scenario YAML file (this will be handled in Ticket 2.2). For this ticket, the capabilities can be hardcoded within the new `LLMJudge` prompt.
* The system for capturing and passing the agent's action `trajectory` to the evaluator (handled in Ticket 2.3). The `LLMJudge` interface will expect it, but the implementation of providing it is separate.
* Persisting the structured results to disk (handled in Ticket 2.4).8b:T1a12,---

### **Epic 1: Scenario Matrix Runner**

**Goal:** Create a robust CLI tool to execute a scenario multiple times across a matrix of different configurations.

* **Ticket 1.1:** [ELIZA-651](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELIZA-651/design-and-implement-scenario-matrix-configuration)
  * **Task:** Define a clear and extensible YAML schema for the matrix configuration file (e.g., `matrix.scenario.yaml`).
  * **Acceptance Criteria:**
    * The schema must support defining a `base_scenario` path.
    * It must support a `runs_per_combination` integer.
    * It must include a `matrix` array where each item specifies a `parameter` (using dot-notation like `character.llm.model`) and a list of `values`.
    * Create TypeScript interfaces for this schema.
    * Implement a validation function using a library like Zod to provide clear errors on misconfiguration.
* **Ticket 1.2:** [ELIZA-652](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELIZA-652/implement-elizaos-scenario-matrix-cli-command)
  * **Task:** Create the new top-level CLI command `elizaos scenario matrix <config_path>`.
  * **Acceptance Criteria:**
    * The command is registered in `packages/cli` using `yargs`.
    * It correctly parses the path to the matrix config file.
    * The command's main function should read, parse, and validate the specified config file.
* **Ticket 1.3:** [ELIZA-653](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELIZA-653/develop-parameter-override-system)
  * **Task:** Implement the core logic that dynamically overrides parameters in the base scenario for each run.
  * **Acceptance Criteria:**
    * Create a utility that takes a loaded scenario object, a dot-notation path (e.g., `run[0].input`), and a value.
    * The utility must correctly set the value in the object, handling nested objects and array indices.
    * This function will be the engine that creates the unique scenario configurations for each matrix run.
* **Ticket 1.4:** [ELIZA-655](https://linear.app/eliza-labs/issue/ELIZA-655/implement-run-orchestration-and-isolation)
  * **Task:** Build the main execution loop that runs all generated scenario combinations.
  * **Acceptance Criteria:**
    * The command must generate a Cartesian product of all matrix parameters.
    * It must loop through each unique combination the specified number of times (`runs_per_combination`).
    * For each run, it must programmatically invoke the existing scenario runner logic with the overridden in-memory scenario object.
    * **Crucially**, ensure each run is completely isolated by cleaning up any generated artifacts (e.g., test databases, temporary files) between executions.

### **Ticket: Develop Parameter Override System**

**ID:** `FEAT-125` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Scenario Matrix Runner`

**Tags:** `cli`, `scenario-testing`, `feature`, `core-logic`

**Estimated Story Points:** `8`

**Dependencies:** `FEAT-123` (Matrix Configuration Schema), `FEAT-124` (CLI Command)

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Implement dynamic parameter override system for scenario matrix testing`

#### **2. Description**

This ticket implements the core logic that enables dynamic modification of scenario configurations based on matrix parameters. The system must be able to take a base scenario object and apply a set of parameter overrides specified using dot-notation paths, creating unique scenario configurations for each matrix combination.

This is the most technically complex part of the matrix runner, as it requires deep object manipulation, array indexing, and robust error handling. The implementation must be flexible enough to handle various scenario structures while being safe and predictable.

The parameter override system will be used by the run orchestration logic (ticket 1.4) to generate the modified scenario configurations for each matrix execution.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **Core Override Function:**
   * Implement `applyParameterOverride(scenario: any, path: string, value: any): any` that:
     * Takes a scenario object (parsed from YAML)
     * Accepts a dot-notation path (e.g., `character.llm.model`, `run[0].input`)
     * Applies the specified value at that path
     * Returns a deep clone of the scenario with the override applied
     * Does not mutate the original scenario object
2. **Dot-Notation Path Parsing:**
   * Support nested object access: `character.llm.model`
   * Support array indexing: `run[0].input`, `plugins[2].enabled`
   * Support mixed paths: `run[0].evaluations[1].value`
   * Handle edge cases like empty arrays, null values, and missing intermediate objects
   * Provide clear error messages for invalid paths
3. **Batch Override Application:**
   * Implement `applyMatrixOverrides(baseScenario: any, overrides: Record<string, any>): any` that:
     * Takes a base scenario and a set of parameter overrides
     * Applies all overrides in sequence
     * Returns the final modified scenario object
     * Handles conflicts gracefully (later overrides take precedence)
4. **Path Validation:**
   * Implement `validateParameterPath(scenario: any, path: string): ValidationResult` that:
     * Checks if a given path exists in the scenario structure
     * Returns detailed information about path validity
     * Suggests corrections for common mistakes (e.g., `run.0.input` → `run[0].input`)
     * Warns about potentially dangerous overrides (e.g., overwriting entire objects)
5. **Type Safety and Deep Cloning:**
   * All operations must work with deep clones to prevent accidental mutations
   * Preserve the original data types when possible
   * Handle special cases like Date objects, RegExp, and other non-plain objects
   * Maintain array ordering and object key ordering where possible
6. **Error Handling:**
   * Clear error messages for invalid paths: `"Path 'run[5].input' is invalid: array 'run' has only 2 elements"`
   * Type mismatch warnings: `"Warning: Setting 'plugins[0]' to string value, but existing value is object"`
   * Path creation for missing intermediate objects: `"Created missing object at path 'character.llm'"`
7. **Performance Optimization:**
   * Efficient path parsing that doesn't recompute for similar paths
   * Minimal object copying - only clone what needs to be modified
   * Caching of parsed paths for repeated operations

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**File Structure:**

```
packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/
├── parameter-override.ts       # Main implementation
├── path-parser.ts             # Dot-notation parsing utilities
├── deep-clone.ts              # Safe deep cloning utilities
└── __tests__/
    ├── parameter-override.test.ts
    ├── path-parser.test.ts
    └── test-scenarios/         # Sample scenarios for testing
```

**Core Types:**

```typescript
interface ValidationResult {
  isValid: boolean;
  error?: string;
  suggestion?: string;
  pathExists: boolean;
  targetType?: string;
}
interface OverrideOperation {
  path: string;
  value: any;
  originalValue?: any;
  wasCreated: boolean;
}
interface OverrideResult {
  scenario: any;
  operations: OverrideOperation[];
  warnings: string[];
}
```

**Path Parsing Examples:**

```typescript
// Should handle these path formats:
parseParameterPath('character.name') 
// → ['character', 'name']
parseParameterPath('run[0].input')
// → ['run', 0, 'input']
parseParameterPath('plugins[1].actions[0].name')
// → ['plugins', 1, 'actions', 0, 'name']
```

#### **5. Example Usage**

```typescript
// Example scenario object

  run: [
    { input: "Hello", evaluations: [{ type: "string_contains", value: "hi" }] }
  ]
};

const overrides = {
  "character.llm.model": "gpt-3.5-turbo",
  "run[0].input": "Hi there!",
  "run[0].evaluations[0].value": "hello"
};
const result = applyMatrixOverrides(baseScenario, overrides);
// Result should have all specified values changed while preserving structure
```

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Design and Implement Scenario Matrix Configuration Schema and Validation`

#### **2. Description**

To enable advanced, multi-variable testing of agent performance, we need a robust way to define a "matrix" of test configurations. This ticket covers the creation of the YAML configuration file that will drive the new Scenario Matrix Runner.

The goal is to design a schema that is both powerful and easy for developers to understand. This configuration will specify a base scenario and a set of parameters to vary, allowing the system to automatically generate and run all possible combinations.

This work is foundational for the entire Scenario Testing and Reporting Framework and does not include the logic for actually running the scenarios, only for defining and validating their configuration.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **YAML Schema Definition:**
   * A formal YAML schema is defined and documented. The configuration file (e.g., `matrix.scenario.yaml`) must support the following top-level keys:
     * `name` (string, required): A human-readable name for the test matrix (e.g., "GitHub Issue Prompt Robustness").
     * `description` (string, optional): A longer description of the test's goals.
     * `base_scenario` (string, required): The file path (relative to the project root) to the base `.scenario.yaml` file to be used in each run.
     * `runs_per_combination` (integer, optional, default: `1`): The number of times to execute each unique combination of parameters to check for consistency.
     * `matrix` (array, required): An array of objects defining the axes of variation. Each object must contain:
       * `parameter` (string, required): The target field in the base scenario to override, specified using dot-notation (e.g., `character.llm.model`, `run[0].input`).
       * `values` (array, required): A list of the values to be substituted for the specified `parameter`.
2. **TypeScript Interfaces:**
   * A new file, `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/src/matrix-schema.ts`, will be created.
   * This file will contain exported TypeScript interfaces (`MatrixConfig`, `MatrixAxis`, etc.) that precisely match the defined YAML schema, ensuring strong type safety for the rest of the feature.
3. **Configuration Validation:**
   * The `matrix-schema.ts` file will also contain an exported validation function, e.g., `validateMatrixConfig(config: unknown): MatrixConfig`.
   * This function will use a library like **Zod** to rigorously validate the parsed YAML object against the schema.
   * Validation errors must be user-friendly and clearly indicate the specific problem in the configuration file (e.g., `Validation Error: "matrix[1].values" must be a non-empty array.`).
4. **Documentation:**
   * A new documentation file, `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/docs/matrix-testing.md`, will be created.
   * This document will explain the purpose of matrix testing and provide a detailed guide on how to create the `matrix.scenario.yaml` file, with clear explanations for each field.

#### **4. Example Configuration**

The documentation and any tests should include a clear example, like the one below:

```yaml

description: "Tests the reliability of listing GitHub issues under various LLM and prompt configurations."
base_scenario: "packages/cli/src/commands/scenario/examples/test-github-issues.scenario.yaml"
runs_per_combination: 3

    values:
      - "List open issues for elizaOS/eliza"

      - "Show me what's open in the elizaOS/eliza GitHub."
```

**ID:** `FEAT-134` (Example ID)

**Epic:** `Performance Reporting Dashboard`

**Tags:** `cli`, `reporting`,- `feature`, `pdf`

#### **1. Title**

`feat(cli): Add PDF export functionality to the 'report generate' command`

#### **2. Description**

To enhance the shareability and archiving of performance reports, this ticket adds an optional PDF export feature. It introduces a `--format pdf` flag to the `elizaos report generate` command, which will generate a high-fidelity PDF version of the HTML report.

This will be achieved by using a headless browser library, **Puppeteer**, to "print" the final, fully-rendered HTML report to a PDF file. This approach ensures that the PDF is a perfect, pixel-for-pixel representation of the interactive web report, including all charts and dynamic content.

#### **3. Acceptance Criteria**

1. **CLI Flag Implementation:**
   * The `elizaos report generate` command is updated to accept a new optional flag: `--format <format>`.
   * The accepted values for `<format>` are `html` (default) and `pdf`.
   * The command's help text is updated to document the new flag.
2. **Puppeteer Integration:**
   * The `puppeteer` npm package is added as a development dependency to the `packages/cli` project.
   * A new utility or service is created to encapsulate the PDF generation logic.
3. **PDF Generation Logic:**
   * When `--format pdf` is specified, the command first generates the final HTML report in a temporary location.
   * It then launches a headless Chrome instance using Puppeteer.
   * It navigates to the temporary HTML file using a `file://` URL.
   * It waits for the page and all JavaScript (including chart animations) to be fully loaded and rendered. A delay or a specific DOM-ready event might be necessary.
   * It calls Puppeteer's `page.pdf()` method to generate the PDF.
   * The generated PDF is saved to the specified output path (e.g., `<input_dir>/performance_report.pdf`).
   * The temporary HTML file is deleted after the PDF is successfully created.
4. **Print-Friendly Styling:**
   * The HTML template is updated with a `@media print` CSS block.
   * These print-specific styles will ensure the PDF output is optimized for a document format:
     * Remove unnecessary UI elements like interactive search/sort buttons from the print view.
     * Ensure chart animations are disabled for printing to capture their final state.
     * Optimize page breaks to avoid splitting charts or tables awkwardly across pages.
     * Set a consistent page margin and font size for readability.

#### **4. Technical Implementation Details**

**Files to Modify / Create:**

* `packages/cli/package.json`: Add `puppeteer` dependency.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/generate.ts`: Add the `--format` flag and the conditional logic to invoke the PDF generator.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/src/pdf-generator.ts`: New file containing the Puppeteer logic.
* `packages/cli/src/commands/report/src/assets/report_template.html`: Add the `@media print` styles.

**Example PDF Generation Logic (**`pdf-generator.ts`):

```typescript
import puppeteer from 'puppeteer';
import fs from 'fs/promises';
import path from 'path';
export async function generatePdfFromHtml(htmlContent: string, outputPath: string): Promise<void> {
  const tempHtmlPath = path.join(path.dirname(outputPath), 'temp_report.html');
  await fs.writeFile(tempHtmlPath, htmlContent);
  const browser = await puppeteer.launch({ headless: true });
  const page = await browser.newPage();
  
  await page.goto(`file://${tempHtmlPath}`, { waitUntil: 'networkidle0' });
  // Optional: Wait for charts to finish animating
  await page.waitForTimeout(2000); 
  await page.pdf({
    path: outputPath,
    format: 'A4',
    printBackground: true,
    margin: { top: '20px', right: '20px', bottom: '20px', left: '20px' }
  });
  await browser.close();
  await fs.unlink(tempHtmlPath);
}
```

**Example Print-Specific CSS:**

```css
@media print {
  body {
    font-size: 10pt;
  }
  .no-print, .interactive-controls {
    display: none !important;
  }
  canvas {
    /* Ensure charts are not split across pages */
    page-break-inside: avoid;
  }
}
```

* Support for any other export formats besides `html` and `pdf`.90:T1b4d,# Dynamic Prompting Implementation for ElizaOS Scenarios

## Overview

Implement **Dynamic Prompting** (multi-turn conversations) in ElizaOS scenarios to enable sophisticated testing of agent behavior through extended conversations where an LLM simulates realistic user responses.

## Problem Statement

Current ElizaOS scenarios are limited to single-turn interactions, making it impossible to test:

* Multi-step problem solving
* Context retention across conversation turns
* Clarification and follow-up question handling
* Natural conversation flow assessment
* Error recovery and correction flows

## Solution

Extend the existing scenario framework to support multi-turn conversations where:

1. **Agent** receives initial user input
2. **Agent** responds with thoughts, actions, and replies
3. **LLM Simulator** generates realistic user follow-up based on agent response
4. **Agent** continues the conversation based on simulated user input
5. Process repeats for specified number of turns or until conditions are met

## Key Requirements

### 1\. Backward Compatibility

* **100% backward compatible** - all existing scenarios must work unchanged
* Gradual adoption path for teams to enhance existing scenarios
* No breaking changes to existing APIs or CLI commands

### 2\. Core Components

#### Schema Extensions

* Extend `RunStepSchema` with optional `conversation` field
* Add new evaluation types: `conversation_length`, `conversation_flow`, `user_satisfaction`, `context_retention`
* Support conversation configuration with user simulator settings

#### User Simulator

* LLM-based response generation with persona-driven prompts
* Configurable personality, objectives, constraints, and knowledge level
* Realistic conversation progression based on agent responses

#### Conversation Manager

* Multi-turn execution orchestration
* Termination condition checking (satisfaction, solution provided, escalation needed)
* Turn-level and final evaluation support
* Conversation transcript generation

#### New Evaluators

* **Conversation Length**: Validate optimal conversation duration
* **Conversation Flow**: Detect required conversation patterns
* **User Satisfaction**: Measure user satisfaction through sentiment analysis
* **Context Retention**: Verify agent memory across conversation turns

### 3\. Configuration Examples

#### Basic Multi-Turn Conversation

```yaml
run:
  - input: "I need help with something"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 4
      user_simulator:
        persona: "polite customer with a billing question"
        objective: "find out why charged twice this month"
        temperature: 0.6
      final_evaluations:
        - type: "llm_judge"
          prompt: "Did the agent successfully help resolve the billing issue?"
          expected: "yes"
```

#### Advanced Persona-Driven Conversation

```yaml
run:
  - input: "This is ridiculous! Your product doesn't work!"
    conversation:
      max_turns: 6
      user_simulator:
        persona: "angry customer who had bad experience"
        objective: "vent frustration but eventually want help"
        style: "initially hostile, gradually becomes cooperative if handled well"
        constraints:
          - "Start with complaints and criticism"
          - "Don't accept first solution immediately"
          - "Become more cooperative if agent shows empathy"
      termination_conditions:
        - type: "user_expresses_satisfaction"
        - type: "agent_escalates_to_human"
```

5. **Documentation**: Clear examples and migration path provided91:T175d,### **Ticket Spec:** `feat(cli): Add 'elizaos scenario run' command`

**Issue Title**: `feat(cli): Add 'elizaos scenario run' command`

**Tags**: `cli`, `scenarios`, `feature`

#### **Description**

This ticket creates the foundational entry point for the Scenario Runner within the ElizaOS CLI. The goal is to register a new top-level `elizaos scenario` command with a `run` subcommand. This command will be responsible for parsing command-line arguments and will serve as the starting point for all future scenario execution logic.

For this initial implementation, the command will perform a "smoke test": it will parse the provided YAML file path, read the file, and print its contents to the console to verify that the command structure and argument handling are working correctly.

#### **Acceptance Criteria**

1. A new top-level command `scenario` is available in the `elizaos` CLI.
2. The `scenario` command has a subcommand named `run`.
3. Running `elizaos scenario run <path/to/scenario.yaml>` successfully reads the specified YAML file and prints its parsed JSON representation to the console.
4. The `run` subcommand accepts a `--live` boolean flag (which will not have any logic yet, but must be recognized).
5. Running `elizaos scenario run --help` displays help text for the command, its positional argument (`filePath`), and its options (`--live`).
6. If the file path argument is missing or the file doesn't exist, the command exits gracefully with an informative error message.

#### **Technical Approach**

The CLI application in `packages/cli` uses the `yargs` library to define and manage commands. We will follow the existing pattern to add our new command.

**1. Create the Command Handler File**

First, create a new file to encapsulate the logic for the `scenario` command.

* **Create file**: `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`

**2. Implement the Command Logic**

Inside `packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts`, we will define the command structure and its handler. We will also need to add a YAML parsing library.

* **Add dependency**: From the root of the `eliza` monorepo, run `bun add js-yaml` to make it available to the CLI package.

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/commands/scenario.ts
import type { Argv } from 'yargs';
import yaml from 'js-yaml';
import fs from 'fs';
import path from 'path';
import { elizaLogger } from '@elizaos/core'; // Assuming a shared logger
// Define the arguments for the 'run' subcommand
interface ScenarioRunArgs {
  filePath: string;
  live: boolean;
}
// Command definition for 'scenario run'
export const command = 'scenario <command>';
export const desc = 'Manage and execute ElizaOS scenarios.';
export const builder = (yargs: Argv): Argv => {
  return yargs.command(
    'run <filePath>',
    'Execute a scenario from a YAML file.',
    (yargs: Argv) => {
      return yargs
        .positional('filePath', {
          describe: 'Path to the .scenario.yaml file',
          type: 'string',
          demandOption: true,
        })
        .option('live', {
          alias: 'l',
          type: 'boolean',
          description: 'Run scenario in live mode, ignoring mocks.',
          default: false,
        });
    },
    (argv: ScenarioRunArgs) => {
      // This is the handler function that executes when the command is called
      handleRunScenario(argv);
    }
  );
};
// The core logic for the command
function handleRunScenario(args: ScenarioRunArgs) {
  elizaLogger.info(`Starting scenario run with args: ${JSON.stringify(args)}`);
  try {
    const fullPath = path.resolve(args.filePath);
    elizaLogger.info(`Attempting to read scenario file from: ${fullPath}`);
    if (!fs.existsSync(fullPath)) {
      elizaLogger.error(`Error: File not found at '${fullPath}'`);
      process.exit(1);
    }
    const fileContents = fs.readFileSync(fullPath, 'utf8');
    const scenario = yaml.load(fileContents);
    console.log('--- Parsed Scenario Content ---');
    console.log(JSON.stringify(scenario, null, 2));
    console.log('-----------------------------');
    
    elizaLogger.info('Scenario file parsed successfully.');
  } catch (error) {
    elizaLogger.error('An error occurred during scenario execution:', error);
    process.exit(1);
  }
}
```

**3. Register the New Command**

Now, we need to wire our new command module into the main CLI entry point.

* **Modify file**: `packages/cli/src/cli.ts` (or the equivalent main `yargs` file where other commands are registered).

```typescript
// packages/cli/src/cli.ts
// ... other imports
import * as scenario from './commands/scenario'; // Import the new module
// ... inside the main yargs setup function
// Find where other .command() calls are made, and add this one.
// It will likely be chained with the others.
yargs
  .command(agent)
  .command(test)
  .command(scenario) // Add our new scenario command module here
  // ... other commands and configurations
```

#### **Testing Strategy**

1. Create a simple `test.scenario.yaml` file in the root of the project:

   ```yaml
   name: "Initial Test Scenario"
   description: "A simple file for testing the CLI scaffolding."
   ```
2. From the `packages/cli` directory, build the CLI changes: `bun run build`.
3. Run the command from the project root and verify the output:

   ```bash
   # Test basic execution
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./test.scenario.yaml
   # Expected output: The JSON representation of the YAML file printed to the console.
   # Test the --live flag
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./test.scenario.yaml --live
   # Expected output: The log line should show "live": true in the arguments.
   # Test help command
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run --help
   # Expected output: Help text showing options for the 'run' command.
   # Test file not found error
   packages/cli/bin/elizaos.js scenario run ./non-existent-file.yaml
